### Travelling projectiles: how EC does them (2026-11-10, end of session)
- **Effect sheets:** every EC fighter's .PL has static effect sheets: type-4 chunks whose tile source (type 0, flag word +12 = 4) is RAW 4bpp tiles.
  - +14 points at the data; +18 is the VRAM area size, e.g. Blade's $92/$94 are 3392 bytes = 106 tiles.
  - Frame record: A-box count + boxes (x1 x2 y1 y2), B count, then pieces: size word ((w-1)<<10 | (h-1)<<8 | $50), attr (tile index + flip/palette bits), x, y; ends with $8000.
  - The offsets table is at sheet +22, relative to +22.
  - Blade's $44 (63 frames: sparkles, balls) and $45 (8 frames: an explosion) decode cleanly.
- **Drawing:** an object spawned with the FIGHTER'S OWN id ($6954 d0 = his id) can show his effect-sheet frames (test PROJ_TEST, now off: a sheet-$44 frame flew out of Raiden's lightning, fine). With the spark's id ($24) the game froze.
- **Projectile hits:** EC's own mechanism (PLYR $19D64), done by the hit engine.
  - The owner's long at $122 is his live projectile. Its $78 is damage, $79 the hit level (table $1A2CC, 16-byte entries), and $36/$3A/$38/$3C its world box, tested against the victim's hurt boxes.
  - `$6A38` (→ PLYR $1ABEC) registers it, a5 = the object with owner at $8C: palette bits from the owner, $94 = the owner's opponent, owner $122 = it.
  - On a hit PLYR calls the object's $84 with d0 (4 = a special case).
  - Xavier's $84 (XAV.CO $2F58) plays sounds, removes the object (bset #2,$82) and clears the owner's $122.
- **Xavier's recipe (XAV.CO $25FC / $33AC):**
  - lea descriptor(pc),a1 (34 bytes, first word = his effect sheet $0049; XAV $2F36); move.w #$1B5A,d0 (object type → $4C); move.w #0,d3; jsr $6BBA (spawn at the owner; d2 = the owner's $98).
  - Then: task code = $33AC, $84 = $2F58 (hit callback), $9A = 4.
  - The task: jsr $6A38 (register); sleep 2; move.l #$60000,d0; jsr $691E (speed 6 px/frame); bclr #1,$7B; move.w #2,$78; then it loops its flight frames.
  - Object type ids by fighter: Blade $1F40-$1F44, Xavier $1B5A/B, Shadow $03E8/9, Chin Wo $4651/4 ... (each fighter its own range).
- **Plan for MK projectiles:**
  - MK's flight art (ice: iceblast poses 14-17; green bolt: greenbolt 10-14; lightning: lightning 14-16, separate from the fighter in the captures) into sheet $45 + tile source $94 of each MK fighter's .PL (≤106 tiles).
  - A descriptor copied from Blade's with sheet $45. Spawn as Xavier does at the cast's release step, our own $84 that removes it and clears $122, MK's speed.
  - The casting moves: plain scripts (like the teleport on $31) instead of Blade's $34 (whose own projectile object would fight ours for $122).
  - Sub-Zero's freeze then triggers on his projectile's hit.

### MK projectiles fly (2026-11-10, evening)
- **Art:** tools/mkprojectile.py cuts the flying part out of MK's capture (components > 40 px in front of him) into his effect sheet **$44** and its raw-tile source (patch '<key>-proj'). Both chunks keep their sizes.
  - Sheet $45 must stay Blade's: EC draws its frames as the impact where a projectile hits (with our art there it showed as stripes).
  - Cast poses lose that part too (scorpion_full.load_action → mkprojectile.strip), so there is no second bolt in his hand.
- **Code** (mkinputs.emit_proj, generic blocks):
  - At his cast's release frame (42:2E) an object is spawned with `$6BBA` (a5 = him, d0 = Blade's type $1F40, a1 = a copy of Blade's descriptor BLA.CO $3B26 with sheet $44). Then its task and $84 hit callback are ours.
  - The task: `$6A38` registers it (his $122); $78 = damage byte : level byte (level 2), $A4 = reaction.
  - EC applies no velocity to it: the task moves it 12 px a run (it runs every other frame = MK's ~6 px/frame) for 35 runs, then removes it and clears his $122.
  - EC's collision box comes from the frame's A box. The hit callback removes it and sets pj_v+2.
- **Values:**
  - Raiden's lightning and Johnny's green bolt: 10 damage, reaction $1A. Verified hitting Shadow from 157 px away.
  - Sub-Zero's ice: 0 damage, no reaction. Its hit starts emit_freeze directly; EC's own brief reaction is overruled for FZ_GRACE calls. Verified frozen from frame 41 to 231 (MK 192 frames), then free to walk.
  - The casts' own strike boxes are off (scorpion_full.no_strike).
- **Next idea (user asked):** Sub-Zero's spine-rip fatality, built like Scorpion's.
  - MK's capture: his grab, pull and head+spine poses.
  - The headless victim from MK drawn in his palette over the EC loser; blood droplets.

## EC's own headless deaths: Z<xx>.BIN (2026-11-13)

Found while looking for art for headless MK fatality victims (the maintainer remembered
EC's shotgun head-blow). tools/ecdeath.py renders them (shots/ec_deaths/).

- **seen** Chicago Theater = LAR files, stage mode $1B (stock disc, forced via GAME.CO
  $C28 once GAME.CO is in RAM). LAR.BGI's finisher checks ($14FA(a6) -> $3400A):
  - +0 (PLYR $17AA4 path, on a hit): loser x $223-$279 (or $170-$1C3 if $100 bit 3)
    -> drive-by Overkill ($1CD4 = 1). If the loser's $159 bit 6 is set instead:
    loser facing right, x $284-$2C4 -> the usher's Sudden Death ($1CD4 = 2).
  - +2 (PLYR $1784C path: loser hp <= 20, $190 >= 10, deciding round, d7 != 0):
    facing right, x $284-$2C4 -> sets $159 bit 6. Not reproduced by a plain hit
    (the $159 bit is rewritten on the hit frame); forced instead by patching LAR.BGI
    in RAM ($34054: bne -> bra), which plays the full usher Sudden Death.
  - The usher scene: drive-by knocks the loser flat, the usher climbs out of the
    booth and shoots the LYING loser's head off (blood spray), stats 'Sudden Death'.
- **seen** The Sudden Death loads LAR.OC/LAR1.OD/LAR2.OD (file ids $84-$86) and then
  a per-victim file at $7C000 via sys_6C26: table LAR.BGI +$284, word per victim
  id-2 = file id $EC-$FD. The runtime file table is at $12E9E (8 bytes per id:
  sector, sectors; built at boot, read it from RAM):
  ZSH ZLR ZRX ZSL ZTR ZMD ZXV ZBD ZJT ZEC(x3 ids) ZRP ZPH ZEX(x3) ZCH(x2) ZRV ZSN ZBS ZDW.
- **seen** Each Z file: two raw-tile sources (flag 4) + two type-4 sheets
  (ids $100+n and $112+n, n = file order; ZEC has only the first).
  - Sheet 1 (~18 frames): the usher kill (lying with head, head burst) and an
    'arched back, torn apart' death.
  - Sheet 2 (~27 frames): frames 0-11 a HEADLESS standing stagger -> knees -> lying
    flat; frames 12-23 the same with the head. All 17 regular EC fighters have it.
  - Palette: the loser's own fighter line (Shadow: line 2 in that capture).
- **seen** LAR.OC's death objects set the frame high byte to a small slot
  (move.b #4,$1A(a5)) and step $1B with sleeps: the resource loader
  (load_resources, list $0014) maps the file's sheets to object slots. Not yet traced.
- **plan** MK decapitation-style fatalities (Sub-Zero spine rip, Johnny's head punch,
  Raiden's head explosion) on EC losers: load the loser's Z file the same way and
  play sheet 2 frames 0-11 on the loser (their own colours: no ghost recolour), his
  held head/spine from the MK capture. MK losers keep MK's captured art.

## New fighter ids instead of hidden slots: survey (2026-11-13, agent, read-only)

The MK fighters currently replace EC's hidden Blast/Chin Wo/Zuni/Yappy. Survey of what
truly new ids would take (the agent's 'seen' = checked in bytes; not re-verified by me):

- Fighter ids are 2..26 (idx = id-2; $AED2 is identity). Object types 27..37 are other
  spawned objects (descriptor table $11E8C has 38 entries), so new fighters need id >= 38
  (idx >= 36).
- Unlock mask $1CC8/$14A4 is one long (saved to backup RAM $762C), tested with btst.l
  (mod 32): idx >= 32 aliases. Better: treat idx >= 25 as always selectable ($20CCC/$1FA3E).
- Hard bounds: GAME $1FA54 cmpi #$19 (25 grid slots); left/right wrap $18; up/down rows
  hard-coded (6, $E, $16; GAME $1FF90-$2011C, ~400 bytes); arcade lists idx 0..22
  ($1EC32, $14DCE, $2263A); portrait offset lsl.w at GAME $21FBE overflows idx >= 35.
- Every id-keyed table has exactly 25 entries and is followed by other data/code: all
  must move. SUB $AED2/$AF04/$AF36 (lea pc at $AE6C/$AE7A/$AEB2: can't reach SUB's end),
  $AF68 (.SND, idx 9 and $18 share slot $17, $AD7E), $B6B8 AI, $B970 HUD, $12A46 win
  names, $12E20 (d8(pc)), $11E8C descriptors; GAME $14C28 stage, $21620 idx->slot,
  $2163A grid (x, y, id; 6 bytes), $21B1C versus names, $22048 palettes (24); PLYR
  $148EC / $15F1B / $153A0 / $18096 (d8(pc) users at $148D8, $1539A, $15EEC); STATS $346B4.
- Space is the real problem: SUB and PLYR have ~10 bytes free each in our build; GAME.CO
  ends $23FDE (guess: free to ~$28000). Tile-source table $D448 (300 x 8 bytes) has only
  5 ids left ($24E-$256), a Blade copy needs 6: move and grow it. Sheets $D9-$FF: ~31 left.
  File-name list $12E9E..$13CF4 (308 entries) is full: new file names need it moved.
- Select-screen icons: GAME $1FA1A..$1FC9C, tile = (-$6320(a6)+$218) + idx*24 (4x6, line 2).
- Steps: (1) find resident free RAM / reclaim SUB space (riskiest), (2) ids 38+ with new
  descriptors, (3) pad and repoint every table, hook the d8(pc) users, (4) unlock rule,
  (5) 4th grid row + up/down rewrite + icons/portraits/palettes, (6) tile sources, sheets,
  sound slots, file names, (7) per-copied-CO id checks (cmpi #9,$4C as the roster patch).
  Untested risk: fighter modules telling objects apart by $4C value.

### Sub-Zero's spine rip on EC's own headless art (2026-11-13, done)

- **done** The fatality loads the loser's Z<xx>.BIN with EC's own loader (`$6C26`,
  -$6E70(a6) = destination, file id $EC + n) over the loser's own .PL ($51800 for P2,
  $81800 Word RAM for P1), from the loser's move task (mkinputs gk_load). It clears
  the loaded-file cache entry -$734C(a6)[id] before (else the loader returns without
  reading) and after (so EC's round-end free loop ignores it). Then MK's music starts:
  a CD data read stops CD audio, so the music must come after.
- **seen** The CD read blocks the whole sub CPU (~1.5 s, ~90 frames): the game pauses.
- **seen** Sheets $100-$1FF: the draw (SUB $F87E) adds $100 to $1A's sheet byte when
  the object's word $28 is non-zero. So the loser's own fighter object shows Z sheet 2
  ($112+n, n >= 10: one less) with $28 = 1, $1A = $12+n:frame, in her own palette.
  Frames used: 0 jerk, 1-5 headless stagger, 6 knees, 7 falling, 8-11 lying
  (mkinputs.szf_zframe, timed from MK's victim capture). She falls toward him.
- **seen** EC's script stepper (PLYR $18174 / $182A6): $AE = the current step (frame
  word -> $1A, +2 ticks -> TCB+$12, +4/+6 dx/dy, +8 -> $F6, +$A -> $102); on expiry
  $C2+1 > $C4 ends the script, else $AE += 12. It runs in TWO tasks of the fighter
  (tasks 3 and 4, PC $182A6), outside the move task (task 2) we park: each may take a
  step after $AE is redirected. A hold = 3 copies of (frame, $7FFF, 0, 0, 0, 0), $C2 = 0,
  $C4 = 2. Without it the loser's dazed script stepped to one of her own frames after
  her .PL was overwritten: EC unpacked garbage and the game hung (seen in the video run
  only, after the freeze/throw segments).
- **done** A fighter's .PL tail: EC loads the whole file but stops parsing at the zero
  chunk length, so data appended after it is resident and untouched (P1 at $81800+off,
  P2 at $51800+off; mkinputs.pl_tail). Sub-Zero's MK attack scripts and the fatality
  tables live there (patches.mk_code, mkinputs.GEN_TAIL / TAIL_FIGHTERS): his code
  block went 5628 -> ~4200 of 5632 bytes. Raiden's CHW.PL has only ~3.4 KB of room.
- Losers without Z art (MK fighters' hosts, the gag animals, the bosses) keep their
  dazed frame. MK-vs-MK FINISH HIM still doesn't reach Sub-Zero's block (open).
- **next** The loser's own severed head: Z sheet 1 frames 6 (upright) and 7 (tumbling),
  every fighter, drawn by a spawned object in the loser's palette at his hand (Sub-Zero)
  or flying up (Johnny's decapitation). The pasted skin-tone head (mkfatality_subzero
  held(), PASTE_HEAD) is off: the maintainer found it looked terrible on Larcen.

### The loser's own severed head in his hand (2026-11-13, done)

- **done** Every EC fighter's head (Z sheet 1 frame 6) is cropped and centred at build
  time (mkinputs.szf_heads): frame records go into Sub-Zero's effect sheet $44 after
  the ice ball's (frames 2+n, tiles numbered from 0), the raw tiles into his .PL tail.
  At the fatality (first timeline call after the Z load) gk_head uploads the loser's
  head tiles over sheet $44's VRAM ($C760 kind 4 -> $CE1A; VRAM address from the
  allocation at -$67D0(a6)+4*src, src = sheet's raw source word / 2) and spawns an
  object (Blade's projectile descriptor, owner HIM) showing $44:2+n with the loser's
  $1E/$20 (her palette line, facing). Each call puts it at his hand (MK capture's head
  blob vs his feet, x1.08, his way round) or off screen (y -256) before he holds it.
- **seen** Gotchas hit on the way:
  - non-fighter objects draw from tiles uploaded once per source ($D3DA: -$6320(a6) =
    VRAM tile base per source id; skips sources already in VRAM), only fighters ($7B
    bit 7) upload per frame: a Z sheet drawn by a spawned object showed garbage;
  - spawning ($6BBA) with the loser as owner, or from inside a task, hung the game:
    spawn from his per-frame code with him as owner (as emit_proj);
  - tail data at an odd address (the 25-byte victim table) = 68000 address errors:
    the tail is padded to even / 32 bytes now.
- EC's own head-frame origins differ per fighter (Larcen's at his chin): centred instead.

  id, sheet 2 - $100) in his .PL tail; gk_load reads them (was n + 1 with EC's file /
  sheet numbering). szf_heads: EC's 18 heads, then the 4 MK heads (mkdeath.held_head:
  MK's art of the head in Sub-Zero's hand). gk clears the loser's $BE (not dazed) when
  the rip starts: Scorpion's own daze wobble kept picking frames from his .PL after
  his death art loaded over it (hang).

  effect sheet $44 (projectile; Sub-Zero's held heads), so in MK vs MK (and vs Blade)
  the later-loaded fighter's $44 won: the head Sub-Zero held over Johnny drew as
  Johnny's green bolt. mkinputs.FX_SHEET gives each his own ($76 Raiden, $77 Johnny,
  $78 Sub-Zero); patches.mk_projectile renames the .PL chunk and Blade's three
  references: his module init's load-resources list (BLA.CO $52: jsr $696C, inline
  $0044 $0045 $FFFF - the sheets whose tiles go to VRAM; without it a renumbered
  sheet drew garbage tiles), move.w #$44,$19C(a5) ($6E), the projectile descriptor
  ($3B26). $45 (the hit explosion, Blade's art in all) keeps its number.
- **found** P1's .CO loads at $1B000 (slot $6800, P2's at $21800). A sector-off load
  ($1B800) was a STALE states/menu.state: EC builds its file table ($12E9E) at boot,
  so a snapshot from before a build that moved files reads the old sectors.
  disc.stale_files / duelstate now refuse a stale menu.state (tools/play.py test
  re-boots and saves a fresh one).

### Mirror matches froze (2026-11-14, fixed)

- **found** Every MK mirror match (both players the same MK fighter) froze within ~2 s.
  Scorpion's already did at the session's start; Raiden's started with 9bc0aaa (his
  scripts moved to his .PL tail). Cause: EC's file cache loads a .PL once, so in a
  mirror match player 2 uses player 1's copy at $81800 - but pl_tail / sco_tail gave
  player 2 $51800 + offset (nothing there): his attack scripts were garbage.
- **fixed** emit_pl_tail: player 2's tail is player 1's copy when both are the same
  fighter. gk_load: the loser's death art goes to $51800 whenever it's a mirror match
  (player 1's .PL is shared with the winner then; $51800 is free).
- Verified: all seven mirror matches fight (60+ s, damage both ways); all seven MK
  fatalities in mirror matches pass (Sub-Zero on his yellow mirror: headless, head held).
- Test suite: one-player scenarios for every MK fighter (07_johnny ... 11_sonya, as
  06_raiden: pick on the grid, versus, stage, fight the CPU with random play): 11 of 11
  pass. MK-vs-MK pairs (7 sampled) fight without freezes.

### MK winner as player 2 (2026-11-14)

- **found** A player-1 fighter can't show player 2's art: Scorpion's fire / skeleton on a
  player-1 victim (Scorpion player 2) drew as scrambled tiles - since his fatality was
  made, unnoticed (every check had him player 1) - and Sonya's burn hung the game.
  The sheet table ($EF4(a6)) and the tile-source table ($D448) are global, so it's in
  how player 1's frames are unpacked (P1's .PL is in Word RAM; the unpacker's slice
  state is global at -$3F6E(a6); DMA commands go to a queue at $BDACE). Flipping the
  victim's $7B bit 0 didn't help. Not solved.
- **worked round** With the MK winner player 2, a victim that would show his art (Sonya's
  burn, Scorpion's fire / skeleton) keeps her own pose and turns black (charred) instead.

### The one-player Contest (2026-11-14, night)

- **tool** tools/checks/contestcheck.py GRID [--matches 6]: Enter Contest, player 1 can't lose,
  the CPU's health is kept low; a still picture gets a Start press (pause, 'Press START'
  screens). A freeze = still 20 s in spite of that. (An earlier scratch harness pressed
  fight buttons on the stats screen and Start mid-fight - two false freezes, Liu Kang's
  and a paused Scorpion. Don't trust a ladder freeze without the sub CPU's PC.)
- **how to see where the sub CPU is:** its registers are in the savestate at
  `find(b'SCD!') + 871490`: D0-D7, A0-A7, PC (little-endian longs), SR (word).
- **found** Scorpion hung at match 7 (vs Midknight): his module (Blade's) loads resources
  $44/$45 and sets $19C = $44 (the effect sheet PLYR.CO gives VRAM at each fight start,
  $1493A / $16FE0), but his .PL has no $44/$45 (strip_sheets). Blade as opponent 1
  registered them in player 2's area ($51800 + $2718C); later opponents' .PLs
  (RAM, SLA, MID) and BIODAT.BIN overwrote that, and in a Contest P1's module isn't
  re-initialised. At match 7 the junk word gave tile-slot index $30EE -> wild code ->
  a corrupt object list -> an endless walk at SUB $C6D6. **done** patches.scorpion_no_fx:
  the list is jumped over and $19C = 0 (PLYR treats 0 as none). The other MK fighters
  have their own effect sheets ($76-$7B, and $45 copies) in their .PLs.
- **found** Raiden and Kano crashed at match 6 (vs Xavier) when Xavier's energy grab
  wrapped round them: XAV.CO $39AE (word per victim id 2-24: the victim's pose, $9A)
  held Chin Wo's 73:67 for Raiden's slot - a frame an MK .PL hasn't got. **done**
  tools/dev/victimtables.py scans every stock module for per-victim frame tables (fixed
  stride, or offset tables like the throws): beside the throw tables there are
  XAV.CO $39AE/$39FA, SLA.CO $20C6 and $2722, JET.CO $326A (8-byte records, two poses),
  MID.CO $293C (10-byte steps; Snake's entry is Trident's: Kano gets a copy at the
  module's end) and MID.OC $52AA (stage finisher, two poses). patches.mk_victim_poses
  gives the MK ids Blade's entry moved to their sheets (as mk_throw_victims).
- First runs (before the fixes): Shadow, Sub-Zero, Sonya, Liu Kang, Johnny: 10 matches,
  no freeze. Scorpion (match 7), Raiden and Kano (match 6) froze.

  Hooter's - the MK fighters' slots. A record: lines `02 00` + 24 characters + `00`,
  a blank line `00 00 00`, 30 blank lines to scroll it off, then `FF 00`.
- **seen** MK CD keeps its endings in RJC.PRG after the bios: per fighter a story page
  and a narrow column (lines end in $FF, $FF $FF = blank; pages end `00 02 14 xx` /
  `00 02 04 xx`).
- **done** tools/mkendings.py reads them (MK's words exactly, its typos too), wrapped to
  24 columns; patches.mk_endings writes them over the hosts' records (in place: they're
  shorter). After the text EC plays its own closing film and credits.
- **ladder shortcut** GAME.CO picks the next opponent from `$1C04(a6)` (incremented at

- Verified: Scorpion beats Eternal and the Dark Champion; his MK text scrolls (readable,
  'V' and ',' fine), then EC's film and credits; no freeze.
- Endings verified (2026-11-14 ~05:00): Scorpion and Raiden by eye; Kano and Sonya by the

- **OPEN** Sub-Zero's ending run crashed in match 1 vs the CPU Riptide (sub CPU back in

## Reproducible from the discs; MK's art decoded (2026-11-14)

- **done** Every capture the build reads is rebuilt from numbers (data/mk_recipes: which
  MK frame, mirrored, where; data/mk_meta: anchors, timings, palettes by fight state):
  233 captures, 3638 poses, pixel for pixel; a disc built from them alone matches the
  working copy's in all 20 files. docs/reproducibility_plan.md has the method.
- **done** MK's fighter art decoded straight from the .OBJ/.SCH (tools/mksch.py: each
  64-byte .SCH cell is a 16x16 sprite, its bytes picking 2x2 pixel groups through two
  256-byte tables in the .OBJ, the longword at $1A; MK frame N is the .OBJ's frame
  N - $10). The frame libraries now come from it (mkframes.ART: which file each fight
  state's player drew from, mirrored for player 2); `mkframes.py --verify`: all ~4200
  frames identical to MK's own drawing, sprite order too (in allchecks).
- **found** Drawing frames by number in the emulator depended on the fight state: the
  idle list's first entry was never overwritten, numbers below $11 leave up whatever
  was showing, a frame could be caught mid-turn; a fatality replay timed from the fight's
  start missed the fatality on a fresh state. The decoder ends all of that; replays now
  count from the first frame BONES.OBJ is drawn from.
- **open** The recorded sounds (fatality effects, the Pit's fall) can't come back byte
  for byte from fresh fight states (recording from the original state does): same to the
  ear for 4 of 9, close for the rest. Left as it is (a judgement call).
- **open** As player 2, Scorpion's and Sonya's fatalities show the loser charred black
  (the worked-round above): player 1 can't show player 2's art. Not solved.
- **found** Sub-Zero's Contest crash (above): the first one was the test's doing -
  contestcheck --ending jumped the ladder to the end between match 1's rounds, so his
  spine rip that won match 1 started EC's end-of-ladder loading mid-fatality. But a
  plain ladder with a fatality on every opponent (`contestcheck.py 15 --fatality
  subzero --matches 14`) freezes too, every time, on the third opponent (Ramses III,
  frame 27262): the fatality's death-art load (gk_load: the loser's Z<xx>.BIN over her
  .PL at $51800, EC's loader $6C26, from her move task) never finishes - the sub CPU
  waits in $C61E for the frame counter $2800A, which its level-2 interrupt (the main
  CPU's per-frame INT2) advances, and the main CPU stops sending it. Skipping the load
  (her task parked at the fatality's start) runs on: no freeze. Not the cause: the pad,
  the stage (the spine rip on Riptide passes on all 18 stages in a 2P duel), distance
  (passes from 273 px), $1CD4 set before the load, loading from his task instead,
  loading 20 frames later, the loser's $2A bit 8 (the same in both modes). Riptide's and
  the second opponent's passed in that run: timing-dependent. Affects every MK fatality
  using the generic code (Sub-Zero, Johnny, Raiden, Liu Kang, Kano) in the one-player
  Contest. Next: what the main CPU waits on (its Work RAM is the state's first 64 KB,
  byte-swapped, from offset 16; its registers not located yet).
- **seen** (2026-11-15) Still freezes on the current build (with the MK dazes: Ramses,
  frame 24774). Not the cause either: the byte $30AAB (1 in a 2P duel, 0 in the
  Contest) set at the fatality's start. EC's own mid-fight loads do nothing more:
  LAR.BGI's Sudden Death loads LAR.OC to $51800 (P2's .PL area, as ours) and LAR1/2.OD
  to $53000 with $6CC6, then the loser's Z<xx>.BIN to $7C000 with $6C26, $14BA(a6) = 1
  (it only says where the .OC went). Main CPU registers: not found in this core's
  state (neither 4- nor 2-byte SR layouts, any alignment); its Work RAM is there.
  Open questions: does EC's own Sudden Death read freeze in the one-player game too
  (then it's the game/emulator, not our code)? What does the main CPU wait on?
- **seen** In a 2P duel the spine rip on Ramses passes with the input 0-76 frames
  after the daze (mkfatalitycheck --wait, 20 timings): not timing within the fight.
  In the Contest it froze on the 3rd opponent after two fatalities; moving the ladder
  first gave other opponents (14, 7, 24) and no freeze in two fatalities.

### The freeze, narrowed: a CPU-controlled loser showing death art (2026-11-15)

- **found** It isn't the Contest or the disc read. A one-player duel reproduces it in a
  minute, every time: `mkfatalitycheck.py subzero --cpu --p2 12` (duelstate.py --cpu:
  player 2 never joins, player 1 picks the CPU opponent; p2=12 gives CPU Raiden, id 19).
  The same spine rip on a HUMAN Raiden passes (`--p2 7`), and on CPU Liu Kang / Kano
  (p2=2, 3) too; most other CPU opponents block the jab (no daze: no test).
- **found** Sequence (states: after the input): the death-art read (our file $137,
  Raiden's MK death art, into $51800) completes in ~95 frames; both move tasks parked
  ($207EA); the fatality timeline runs (his frames $DE52, $DE53); at frame 256 the loser
  shows death-art frame $1E03 ($28 = 1: sheet $11E); at frame 261 the sub CPU is reset
  (PC $2E0, A7 $5E80, registers cleared) - only the main CPU can reset it: the MAIN CPU
  dies drawing/transferring the CPU-controlled loser's first death-art frame. (The
  Contest's 'stalled read' was the same: after the main CPU stops, the sub's frame
  counter no longer ticks.)
- **seen** The CPU flag is the fighter's word +$12A ($FF00 CPU, 0 human: our input hook
  at $1AEF6 ends with `tst.w $12A(a1)`, then PLYR $15114 runs the AI and writes a pad
  word). Clearing it at the fatality's start doesn't prevent the crash; nor parking a
  task at $2C4B6 (-> $40B02, AI code) the way move tasks are parked. Both losers'
  $2A = $4000 (bit 8 clear: the sub CPU unpacks) - not the unpacker choice.
- **next** What the main CPU does with that frame for a CPU-controlled fighter (the
  sub's transfer queue, $FC70 / $116A4, docs/research/p2_fatality_animation_research.md), or
  which other per-fighter state differs for a CPU fighter (+$12C = AI routine $415D2,
  $2E45C; $2F888 -> $43355).
- **found (root cause)** The fighter's word +$15E is EC's daze loop counter: the
  fighter modules set it to 100 with the daze ($BE = $1F, e.g. $1C024), and EC's script
  stepper (PLYR $182B4) counts it down and, while it's non-zero, puts the fighter's
  script back to her saved one ($15A / $160: her daze). Our fatalities cleared $BE but
  not $15E. A CPU loser's AI re-dazes her (at the fatality's start her $15E was 98; a
  human's had run out), so after the death art was read over her .PL the stepper put
  her own daze frames back - drawn from the replaced .PL: the main CPU died (and the
  Contest's 'stalled read' was the same death a little earlier). Clearing only $15E at
  the fatality's start (of all the fields a CPU fighter has and a human hasn't) lets it
  finish.
- **fixed** Both fatality starts (the generic gk start and Scorpion's r_fatal) clear
  the loser's $15E with $BE. `mkfatalitycheck subzero --cpu --p2 12` passes.
- **found** Two more ways a CPU loser broke a fatality (Contest with --fatality: Kano
  on Trident, Johnny on Eternal): her daze loop counter set again AFTER the fatality's
  start (Eternal: 99), and a move request on her ($BE = $8000 with $100 bit 0, from her
  AI; EC's dispatcher then started her own move 2: her move task -> her module's $22B04,
  her own frames). Clearing her $12A or holding her step count ($C2) didn't help.
- **fixed** Every fatality call (gk_step, f_step) keeps the loser's $15E at 0 and
  cancels a move request on her (clears $BE when bit 15 is set, and $100 bit 0). Kano's
  Contest then runs on past Trident.
- **seen** After the fix, the full Contest with a fatality on every opponent passes for
  Scorpion, Raiden, Sub-Zero, Sonya, Liu Kang, Kano. Johnny's (24 fatalities) stops
  after Eternal: he beats Eternal with an ordinary hit (frame 127157, no daze, no
  fatality on Eternal), the game loads the next fight's files ($40800-$43000, the AI
  area) and dies in that load (sub CPU reset, ~128310). Without fatalities his ladder
  and his ending run pass. Suspects: what his earlier fatalities leave behind - the
  flying head object, or EC's loaded-file list after the death-art loads (gk_load
  clears an entry before and after its read).
- **found** Johnny's: with his fatality attempts capped (contestcheck --fatality-max) at
  12, 20, 21, 22 or 23 the whole ladder passes; at 24 it stops after Eternal. Attempts
  11-23 were all on opponent 8, where his fatality never took (no finisher: she stays
  dazed); the 24th is his fatality on Riptide (14), the last opponent before Eternal -
  that's the one. Not EC's loaded-file list (-$734C(a6): RIP.*'s entries hold $29190
  while loading and are 0 before the fatality; clearing RIP.PL's changed nothing).
  contestcheck --fatality-on IDS, EK_VERBOSE=1 (each attempt).
- **open** Why Johnny's fatality never takes on opponent 8 (13 attempts, dazed).
- **seen** Johnny's, continued: his fatality only on Riptide passes; with it plus any
  one third of the earlier ones (3 bisect groups) passes too: only the exact full run
  (24 attempts) stops. The current test needs `--fatality-immediate` to reproduce
  the original input timing. From that run's checkpoint at frame 127200 (Eternal beaten,
  $BE $7FFF) the stop is fixed whatever the pad does: +1028 a file is read into $40800
  (the AI area; EC's loaded-file list index 311, not one of SUB.BIN's 308 names), the
  read reaches $43000, then the sub CPU is reset (+1111). Just before the read, tasks in
  $2C24A-$2C584 point into the AI area ($40B92, $40FFC, $41038, $41182, $41A88 ...):
  likely tasks still running from the area being overwritten. Next: the same moment in
  a passing run (after Eternal is beaten, not her mid-fight form loads) to compare
  which tasks are left; contestcheck --rewind and EK_VERBOSE help.

- **found (Johnny reset, immediate cause)** The file in that last load is
  `CHX.AI` (the bytes at $40800 match the built disc's complete $29E0-byte
  file). EC reads whole $800-byte sectors, so its sixth sector also writes
  padding through $437FF. The loader's stack is nearby: its SP is $43854
  while it waits for that sector. A level-4 interrupt arrives just after the
  BIOS enables interrupts; its register save and nested calls push the stack
  below $43800. In the failing checkpoint a trap at $1550 shows SP $437F6
  and the expected return address $1338 has become $00000000. The DMA has
  zeroed that address, so returning resets the sub CPU. This also explains
  the timing sensitivity: a 23-attempt passing control loads the same final
  sector with the same stack location but survives.
- **probes, not fixes** Redirecting just the last sector to another PRG RAM
  location avoids the immediate reset, but does not deliver the intended
  file. Holding the BIOS at $1DC2 with interrupts masked until the DMA has
  finished, then restoring the instruction, also continues past the reset.
  Neither is a proposed shipping patch. Parking the saved AI task PCs before
  the read did not prevent the reset; those pointers alone are not its cause.
  Need a safe fix for the overlap, including the nested-interrupt stack and
  other files/loads, then rerun the full 24-attempt Johnny ladder and the
  existing fatality and ending regressions.
- **candidate to investigate** In a checkpoint-only experiment, replacing the
  BIOS return at $1DC2 with a trampoline that waits for bit 7 of $FF8004 before
  restoring SR also got past the reset. A trampoline with one or three NOPs
  instead still reset. This is not yet a fix: the flag's semantics and behavior
  for other BIOS calls, modes and drives need verification, and the experiment
  patched running RAM only. Waiting on bit 6 instead never completed in this
  experiment. A final patch must not silently hang other disc operations.
- **candidate patch, under test** `cd-ai-stack` in `tools/patches.py` changes
  the EC loader's CDC-read call. For a sector destined for $43000 it saves SR,
  masks interrupts, makes the BIOS call, and on success waits for the same
  $FF8004 bit 7 the original loader waits for, before restoring interrupts.
  It restores the BIOS condition codes for the loader's error handling; other
  sector destinations take the original call. It does not write to protected
  BIOS memory. On the failing checkpoint, the CHX.AI sector's 480 meaningful
  bytes were present when the bit became ready, and the next fight loaded.
  A full 24-attempt Johnny reproduction passed with this code injected into
  the running game (`EK_VERBOSE=1 python tools/checks/contestcheck.py 19 --fatality
  johnny --fatality-immediate --matches 14 --frames 145000`, with the original
  menu state). A fresh disc build succeeded, and Johnny and Sub-Zero
  each reached the 15th opponent in fresh-boot Contest tests without a freeze.
  Those runs logged 11 and 12 fatality attempts respectively, so they are not
  proof that every opponent's fatality completed. Real hardware remains untested.
  The helper uses 52 bytes at
  the end of SUB.BIN, leaving only 10 bytes below PLYR.CO in this build.
  Sega's [Mega-CD hardware manual](https://segaretro.org/images/5/51/Mega-CD_Hardware_Manual.pdf)
  identifies this bit (bit 15 of the word at $FF8004) as EDT: the gate array
  sets it when the CDC transfer ends, independent of software handling an
  interrupt. The earlier note calling it an unverified ready flag is superseded.
- **Xavier fatality check** The opponent with id 8 is Xavier. Johnny's fatality
  succeeds on him in both CPU and two-player duels (`mkfatalitycheck.py johnny
  --p2 6 --cpu`, and without `--cpu`). In the long Contest run, at Xavier's
  first daze Johnny is still in move/recovery $BE=$1C; the test sends its
  fatality while he cannot accept it. At the same saved moment, waiting 65
  frames for his $BE to clear before the test's normal input produces a full
  finisher ($1CD4=3, Xavier's health 0). The test now waits for the winner to
  become neutral; `--fatality-immediate` retains the prior input timing for
  reproducing the original 24-attempt freeze. The fighters were far apart,
  but approaching was not needed in this experiment. With the revised tester
  and the disc patch injected, the original ladder reached opponent 26 without
  freezing, with 12 input attempts instead of 24. A fresh-boot Johnny ladder on
  the built disc also reached opponent 26 without freezing (10 input attempts).
  Input-attempt counts do not prove that each finisher completed.

- **fixed (Johnny reset)** `cd-ai-stack` is in the build (data track SHA-1
  ad85dbe8...). On it, the 24-attempt Johnny run (`contestcheck.py 19 --fatality
  johnny --fatality-immediate --matches 14 --frames 145000`) reaches opponent 26.
  Control from the same menu.state with only the call at SUB $6220 put back
  (303c008b4eb85f22) freezes at frame 129428, after opponent 11, as before: the
  patch is what passes it. CHX.AI ($29E0), CHP.AI ($2AA0) and TRID.AI ($29C8) are
  stock EC sizes: all three reach the $43000 sector, so the hazard is EC's own,
  our changes only moved the timing. Note: states made before a build hold the
  old SUB.BIN in PRG RAM; re-save menu.state (allchecks does) before testing.
- **open (Dark Champion lock)** allchecks after that build: 50 of 52; Sub-Zero's
  and Sonya's Contest endings stop in the Dark Champion fight with both fighters
  still (not a freeze: the game runs, Start does nothing). Same with the patch
  reverted, so not cd-ai-stack. Sub-Zero's (grid 15 --ending, frame 52059 on):
  the Dark Champion's move $2C (code at $24FCE in his module) spawns his drain
  object (type $5DC9, kept in his $122) at the player, clears his $A2 and waits
  (loop at $25084) for $A2 != 0. The object's message handler ($2637E: d0 != 4 =
  removal) sets the owner's $A2 = 1; d0 = 4 is the contact that grabs the player
  (his $100 |= 2, $10E = $64). In the stuck state the object ($2D85A) still exists
  and is still in his $122, but its task slot ($2C4EE) is freed: it was ended
  without its handler, so $A2 never comes. Just after the grab (frame 52080)
  Sub-Zero's $BE gets move requests ($8004, $8009, then $7FFF at 52098): MK
  inputs acting while EC holds him, like the CPU loser in the fatality freeze.
  Not the test's health pinning (topping up instead still stuck, hp 99). Without
  pinning, three drains on Sub-Zero ended normally (each took 20).
  Correction and more: the object's task is not freed (an object's $88 points at
  its task, resume at +4: $2648E). After the contact (frame 52105: his $100 |= 2,
  $10E = $64, which then stops at $63) the object lifts its $94 = the player
  ($2E188) by $10 in $52 every 5 frames until $52 >= $150 (code at $2648E), then
  drains. His $52 never gets there: it goes $E8, $F8, $108 and back to $E8, again
  and again; something resets his height. He was walking ($BE 8, his task parked
  at $1C8A0 in EC's walk loop) when the contact came, one frame after the walk
  started. Clearing his $100 bit 5 (left set by his down+X move) changes nothing.
- **fixed (Dark Champion lock)** The writer: EC's pose setup in SUB.BIN ($11754,
  $11E7A: a new pose puts a fighter at $B6 + the pose's dy, $52 = ...). Found by
  blanking every write to $52 in the stuck state but the drain's own ($26492): with
  all blanked the lift reaches $158; only those two matter. Our MK walk (and daze)
  drive sets a new pose every few frames even while EC holds him; EC's own walk
  doesn't run then (his task waits), so his pose stays and the lift goes up. Fix:
  the walk/daze drive skips a fighter whose $100 bit 1 (EC's held-by-a-grab flag:
  throws, the drain) is set (mkinputs: Scorpion's d_ block and the generic g_
  block). Sub-Zero's and Sonya's Contest endings pass again.
- **fixed (player-2 Scorpion/Sonya fatality art)** As docs/research/p2_fatality_animation_research.md
  proposed: a player-1 victim's poses go to the main CPU ($2A bit 8, SUB $1178E),
  which can't reach player 2's art. mkinputs now clears that bit when the winner
  (player 2) gives her a pose of his art, and sets it again for her own (Scorpion's
  charred step). With both fighters unpacked by the sub CPU, its one in-progress
  job (-$3F6E(a6)) garbled the first flame step: her new pose now waits one call
  when his pose changes on the same call. Fight start resets $2A ($4100 again in
  the next fight: mkfatalitycheck --after). Remaining: a one-frame flash of her own
  pose in his colours before the flames, on both players' sides (palette applies
  at once, the pose a frame later).
- **fixed, player 1 (Sonya's kiss)** MK's kiss rises (poses 13, 17, 21), is off MK's
  screen for 32 frames (her pose 10), then drops onto the victim (52, 56). MK's screen
  ends ~140 px above her feet, ours ~200, so it vanished in mid-air. Now an object
  (mkinputs.emit_kiss: her effect sheet's kiss piece, cut from pose 21) flies it in
  that gap on one arc (kiss_path: up at its rising speed, down into pose 52's place);
  the burn rows carry $8000 + n for it. It has its own slot (gq_obj): the head-object
  slot gk_z+2 holds another object on player 2's side.
- **open (Sonya's kiss as player 2)** With the kiss object, player 2's fatality never
  burns the victim: the sub CPU crashes (PC $5EE) after the drop, her BE 0, $2A still
  $4100. Not the tile upload (skipped: same), not the object kept hidden (deleted with
  $6A14 at the drop: same), not the code size (the routine there, not called: fine).
  Player 2 doesn't show it for now (gq_kiss returns): the kiss vanishes, as before.
- **fixed (thrown projectiles cut the thrower in half)** An MK pose's anchor is the centre
  of its top 12 rows (mkmoves.anchor). In Liu Kang's fireball (poses 16-24), Kano's knife
  (16-43) and Sonya's rings (16-26) the projectile flew higher than the head, so the
  anchor followed it 80-120 px ahead and the crop around it cut his legs off (the maintainer
  saw half of Liu Kang vanish). scorpion_full.load_action re-anchors a pose on its head
  once the projectile is stripped.
- **fixed (flying moves on the floor)** Liu Kang's flying kick and Kano's cannonball are
  airborne in MK (60-70 px up) but every pose's feet went to the floor (he slid along
  it). Now in KEEP_HEIGHT (MK's height over the stance), capped so a pose's top stays
  within MAX_UP (not cut). Raiden's torpedo keeps its old uncapped anchors (capped, his
  .PL went 684 bytes over MAX_PL).
- **open** Sonya's square-wave flight: she doesn't leave the ground in showcase.py (the
  hit lands); KEEP_HEIGHT made no difference - something else.
- **open (Kano's knife throw cut off)** His knife poses had the same bug (anchor on the
  knife: poses 18-24 were 6-63 pieces of him instead of 93). Re-anchored, his one-player
  Contest crashed the sub CPU (PC $5E8, frame 55574, vs fighter 2, Kano idle frame
  $E501) every time; without it, it passes (108191 frames, as before). His file stayed
  under MAX_PL. Excluded for now (scorpion_full.load_action: name != 'knife').

### Sonya: burn frames and square wave (2026-11-15)

- The previous Sonya fatality check only checked the finisher flag and health.
  Both player sides passed while the victim remained standing. `mkfatalitycheck.py`
  now also requires the victim to display fire and skeleton frames; it fails on
  the previous build on both sides.
- Kiss-path markers occupy $8000-$80FF. The fatality and kiss-object routines
  used the sign bit to recognize them, but the actual fire/skeleton sheet words
  also have that bit set (Sonya's sheet starts at $E9). Restrict marker handling
  to the marker range. A Shadow victim now visibly burns and becomes a skeleton
  with Sonya on either side; both duels also progressed into another fight.
  The player-2 kiss object was enabled again, but needs broader testing. A
  player-2 Sonya mirror duel did not trigger the fatality, on either the old or
  new build; investigate separately.
- Square wave used the ground-anchored art and EC dash's held frames $41:61/62
  were mapped to Sonya's landing pose. Preserve the MK captures' height and map
  those two frames to her airborne approach and punch. Real pad-input duels on
  both sides showed the punch and inflicted damage. Movement still uses EC's dash.
- Sonya's leg grab is absent from the special-move handler. The MK capture has
  the poses, but adding the move requires its input, grab and victim choreography;
  do not claim it exists based solely on the captured art.
- The female Pit track is deliberately assembled with `GIRLHURT.SVX` (a short
  cry). The longer fall sample in the current build is used for the male track;
  the original MK female fall sound has not been verified.

### Kano move review (2026-11-15)

- `shots/kano_moves.mp4` was generated by `showcase.py`: the knife input starts
  at 19.3 s, the cannonball input at 22.4 s, and the throw at 30.4 s. The knife
  animation has the known body-cropping defect. The cannonball uses Blade's dash
  and Scorpion's teleport frame assignment; 41:61/62 map to capture poses 4/5,
  before the cannonball spin poses in `mk_moves_kano/cannonball`. The MK capture
  has the rolling poses, but the dash does not reproduce their full sequence.
- MK Kano's close punches are headbutts. The capture set has no headbutt action,
  and `mkscripts.MOVES` maps the close punch to the generic `btn_x` action. To
  add it correctly, capture the close MK headbutt and map the applicable close
  moves, then verify their hit range and both player sides.

### Mirror matches: player 2's fatality never started (2026-11-15)

- **fixed** In a mirror match of a generic MK fighter (all but Scorpion) won by
  player 2, his fatality input did nothing: the finishing taps came out as an
  ordinary attack and KO'd the dazed loser ($1CD4 0). Seen with Sonya (Codex)
  and Kano; Scorpion's worked (one full block serves both his sides).
- Cause: both slots load his module, so both carry his block with its own vars.
  r_finish is a fight-wide hook; the PLYR stubs call the first block found,
  player 1's. It only asked "is the winner this block's fighter?" - yes for
  either Sonya - so it opened the FINISH HIM window (vars+52) in player 1's
  block, while player 2's r_drag / gk_ input read player 2's block's vars (none).
- Fix: r_finish(full=False) in generic blocks also asks `mine` (is the winner
  in this block's slot); if not, fi_fwd hands it to the other slot's block, as
  for two different MK fighters.
- Checked: every MK fighter's mirror fatality, both sides, stage $11: 14 of 14.
  allchecks gains "mirror fatality (P2)" for each. On Sonya's own stage (the
  Pit bridge) the test's jab knocked the dazed loser off the bridge - the
  stage's fall, not a fatality bug - hence stage $11 in the check.
- With Codex's marker-range fix the player-2 kiss object runs: Sonya as player 2
  vs select-grid opponents 0 (Shadow), 3, 8, 12 and herself (16): flames and skeleton
  seen, no crash. The old player-2 crash (PC $5EE "after the drop") fits the old
  sign test: once the drop ended, her real burn frames ($E9xx) were taken as kiss
  positions ($69xx into the gq_pos table).

### Kano and the original female Pit fall (2026-11-15)

- Captured Kano's close low-punch headbutt on the original MK disc. Its 19
  captured poses have exact disc-derived recipes, including its three action
  poses. `mkscripts` assigns that animation to both EC close punch scripts. In
  direct duels both punches hit as either player, with the headbutt visible.
- Re-anchoring Kano's stripped knife poses restores the missing parts of his
  body. A candidate containing this and the headbutt completed a ten-opponent
  Contest and a 14-opponent Contest with eight fatality attempts, without a
  freeze. An older re-anchored build froze in a different run, so this does not
  prove all Contest timings safe. The previous showcase's knife input actually
  launched EC's other attack; back, forward, then Z triggers the actual knife.
- Original-MK female Pit verification: selected Sonya as P1 and Sub-Zero as
  P2, advanced through the two-player stage cycle, and uppercut Sonya off the
  Pit. The captured final frame shows her on the spikes. Repeating with MK's
  CD music muted isolates the long falling effect; its waveform matches the
  male Pit recording during the long scream (several quarter-second windows
  correlate above 0.96 after a one-frame timing alignment). MK uses the same
  long scream for Sonya. The build now uses the disc-reconstructed fall track
  for both genders rather than substituting GIRLHURT for women.

- Sonya leg-grab research: the whole-screen MK capture confirms that she turns
  upside down, clamps the victim between her legs, and throws them behind her.
  Added exact disc recipes and metadata for the existing 23-pose capture so
  that work can use it without the emulator. A prototype routing the chord
  through EC's throw either played the ordinary throw or left Sonya stuck;
  that prototype was removed. The victim's choreography still needs its own
  implementation and interrupt/recovery tests.
- Square-wave measurements: MK's captured startup rises from ground to roughly
  116 screen pixels above it and then travels forward; the current EC dash
  moves Sonya's game-position Y at floor height throughout, relying on raised
  sprite anchors to look airborne. A correct fix needs takeoff and landing,
  real hitbox positioning, and interrupted-move recovery, not just more raised
  art. It remains open.

### Kano's knife throw: MK's windup and a spinning knife (2026-11-15)

- Blade's cast ($34) shows 20 frames, 2 game frames each, in this order: 40:60-63,
  42:28-2D, 42:2E 2F 2E 2F (the object flies at the first 42:2E), 42:30, 42:31,
  42:30, 40:5E 5F, 42:32-35 (some twice), then a walk back to 40:60.
- Spread evenly over MK's 45 knife poses (scorpion_full.pick, cap 8) the first five

  the throw itself (10 crouch, 11-12 reach back, 13 overhead, 14-15 slash with the
  trail, 16-17 release) mostly fell between frames. Pinned now (patches.KANO extra

  25, 26, 28, 30, 32.
- The flying knife showed only poses 25/27 (both the flat blade) and appeared a
  few frames after it left his hand (MK's spinning crescents 18-24 were stripped
  from his poses and never flown). The object now alternates crescent 21 and
  blade 25 (mkprojectile.PROJ, every 4 runs): it spins, and with the release pinned
  to pose 17 there is no gap. Checked both sides at range: windup, slash, release,
  spin across, hit.
- MK's input (Block held: back, forward) still only blocks: while Z is held EC's
  block state (move 3) keeps the taps from the special-move check (r_taps' CHORD
  path never runs). Back, forward, then Z throws (both sides; probed). Open.

### Teleport visual fixes (2026-11-16)

- Raiden's teleport left an animated electric-looking object on Shadow without
  doing damage, reproducible on a fresh disc and on more than one stage. This
  was Blade's own move-$31 task spawning its projectile at BLA.CO $2534, even
  though Raiden's animation script replaced Blade's. Its object continued on
  Blade's $3B96/$3C24 tasks and followed Shadow. `patches.mk_code` skips that
  spawn in Raiden's module and resumes normal move recovery. Independent Raiden
  lightning attacks still hit for 10 and his fatality completes as P1 and P2.
- Scorpion's incoming punch and landing art were not complete: the saved MK
  capture drew frame 128 at x=-49 and frame 17 at x=-24, clipping the parts
  outside the original screen. The recipes now draw those complete disc frames
  120 px farther into the capture canvas and move their anchors by the same
  amount. His incoming punch is up to 80 px from its anchor, so TELE_EDGE is
  now 238 (82 px from the opposite screen edge). Tested near both screen
  edges: the whole arrival is visible and the punch still does 17 damage.
- `teleportcheck.py` exercises the moves as either player, checks that Raiden
  creates no stray effect and Scorpion's restored art reaches the target. Both
  checks fail against the old disc and pass against the patched disc. Ten-match
  Contest runs with repeated fatalities completed for both fighters; broader
  scenarios should still be run before release.

### Kano's held-Block knife input (2026-11-16)

- With Z held through back, forward, EC's recognizer really does accept the
  knife: the fighter's $11A bit 11 sets and the input object at $184 contains
  $34 at +$42 and a queued request at +$46. Kano is already in move 3, whose
  task waits in Blade CO $17C4 without checking that request, so it expires.
- Kano's generic per-frame routine now checks only human Kano in move 3 with
  that recognized $34 request. It redirects his move task to a small routine
  that enters Blade's own special dispatcher at CO $2206 with d0=$34. The
  dispatcher clears the input queue and starts the normal knife script. This
  leaves other specials and CPU fighters on their existing paths.
- `kanoknifecheck.py` requires Z held throughout back, forward, no premature
  knife, and damage to the opponent. It fails on the earlier disc and passes
  on the patched disc for both players, also in Kano mirrors. Kano's fatality
  still completed as both players. His full Contest run reached the end
  without freezing, with 10 fatality attempts across 14 opponents.
- A separate clean directory with only the EC and MK discs, the project data,
  and tools (no BIOS or emulator states) ran prepare and build. All 20 output
  files match the tested combined disc byte for byte. In this sandbox the
  decoder's multiprocessing process mode was set to `fork` in the temporary
  copy because the default forkserver socket is not permitted.
- The combined candidate passed 39 of 39 quick checks (including new P1/P2
  teleport and held-Block knife tests). The temporary test copy used `fork`
  for the decoder check because this sandbox rejects forkserver sockets.

### Trident's Vendetta on MK fighters (2026-11-16)

- Trident was blocked against MK fighters. Forcing his Vendetta against Raiden
  starts it but crashes: TRI.VC's 16-byte victim record for Chin Wo requests
  frames $7365 and $7367, which Raiden's MK art does not contain.
- `mk-victim-poses` now copies Blade's TRI.VC victim record for each MK slot,
  shifting its four frame words to that fighter's sheets. Trident is allowed
  by the per-opponent Vendetta switch. His blood spray and whirl were compared
  with his Vendetta on Shadow; the original effect also includes blue/grey.
- All seven MK victims reached final statistics with Trident on both player
  sides, using the actual build's switch. A CPU Raiden also reached statistics
  with CPU control restored when the Vendetta began (the harness suppressed
  the AI during setup). The old disc reproduces the Raiden freeze when forced.
  `vendettacheck.py` now checks for statistics, supports player 2, and returns
  failure if the finisher freezes. Both sides of Trident on Raiden are included
  in `allchecks.py`.

### Midknight's and Blade's Vendettas on MK fighters (2026-11-16)

- **found** Midknight's Vendetta has its own victim steps in MID.VC: a 66-byte record
  per loser id - 2 at $A1E (eight 8-byte steps: frame, dx, dy, flags into $20; then
  $FFFF; MID.VC $970 indexes it by $4C). Liu Kang's, Kano's, Sonya's, Johnny's and
  Sub-Zero's slots are all zeros (the loser vanished while he lifted nothing), Raiden's
  holds Chin Wo's frames (73:67: hang), Scorpion's Blast's. `mk-victim-poses` now
  copies Blade's record to each MK slot, moved to his sheets (as Trident's TRI.VC).
- **found** Blade's Vendetta (allowed on every MK fighter since all seven have death
  art) hung on six of them. It picks its grab by loser id: `jmp 2(pc,d0)` at BLA.VC
  $4D8 into a bra.w per id (from $4DC), each starting a case that writes the loser's
  frame ($1490(a6) -> $1A). The hidden animals' ids point at $BC8, data (ILLEGAL):
  Liu Kang, Kano, Sonya, Johnny, Sub-Zero hung the game at the grab; Raiden ran Chin
  Wo's case (hang), Scorpion Blast's. `mk-blade-vendetta` rewrites Blast's case ($ADC,
  only Scorpion's slot reached it) as one for all seven: Blade's frame 3D on sheet 2
  of the loser's own (a byte per id), and points their entries at it. His second
  pose table (BLA.VC $B68, two frames per loser) is in VICTIM_POSES too.
- `victimtables.py` missed these three (Trident's included): strides only to 40, and
  zero records counted against a table. Now strides to 80, zeros neutral; it finds
  TRI.VC $746, MID.VC $A1E, BLA.VC $B68 (BLA.VC's jump table is code: not a table).
- Midknight added to VENDETTA_WINNERS. Verified with the build's own switch
  (`vendettacheck.py --keep-block`, to the final statistics): Trident, Midknight and
  Blade on all seven MK fighters as player 1 and player 2 (Blade on Shadow as player 2:
  the harness's input isn't taken, kind 0 - not an MK problem). Midknight lifts the MK
  fighter overhead and drops him as he does Blade (MK fighters use Blade's poses).
  allchecks: Midknight on Raiden and Blade on Sonya, both sides.
- **seen** During Midknight's bite some losers vanish from the sprite list while
  their object still holds the lying frame: Shadow (stock) and Scorpion; Blade, Sonya,
  Raiden stay. Same on an EC victim: left.
- vendettacheck.py: `--frames` (Midknight's runs past 900), `--trace` (both
  fighters' frame, state; the loser's x, y, $28 per change), `--save-at N --save`.

### The one-frame flash before the burns, fixed (2026-11-16)

- **found** (mkfatalitycheck.py --trace: the loser's $1A/$1C, her palette line, EC's
  palette flag, each frame) The fatality timeline runs every OTHER frame (with r_generic).
  A pose set on frame N is drawn ($1C) on N+1 and seen on N+2; colours copied into
  her palette line on N are seen on N+1 (EC copies -$6956 to -$69D6 the same frame when
  -$67D4 is set, SUB $D0F2/$D26C; uploaded at the vblank). So her own pose showed in his
  colours for a frame. Copying a call later only moved it (the new pose in her own
  colours for a frame): no call lands on N+1.
- **seen** A sprite's palette line comes from the object's $20 bits 13-14, applied when
  its sprites are next built: a change on N is seen on N+2, as a pose.
- **fixed** The victim's sprites now take the WINNER's palette line ($20 bits 13-14 =
  his) on the call that sets his art's pose (Scorpion's f_p1v, the generic burn for
  Sonya), instead of copying his colours into her line. Scorpion's charred step puts
  her back on her own line (f_line) as it blacks it. Checked frame by frame: Scorpion
  and Sonya, both sides, straight from her pose to the flames. Her $20 is EC's again in
  the next fight ($8000 / $A800; mkfatalitycheck --after now prints both).

### Sonya's square wave rises and drops (2026-11-16)

- **seen** (per-frame trace) Blade's dash script (BLA.CO $4F74, steps of 12 bytes from

  the air): she popped up in one frame and stood back on the floor in one.
- **done** patches.SQUARE_STEPS: in her module's copy the steps show 41:30 (MK pose 10,
  the crouched launch), 41:31 (11, tucked rising), 41:61 (12), 41:62 (13, the punch),
  41:32 (17, dropping upright), 41:34 (18, the landing crouch). 41:30-34 are in no Blade
  script (only Scorpion's own teleport steps show them). MK's capture holds pose 18 in
  the air for 14 samples, then lands in 19: ours holds it for the last 2 ticks, then

  the mover ($69D8: x += $5E, y += $62, SUB $10A4E) and copies back. Poking $52 alone
  is undone the next frame; with $B6 too she stays up. $1496(a6) is the floor ($17002).
- **done** mkinputs.emit_sq_lift (Sonya's block, each call): in her dash (move $35) her
  $B6/$52 = the floor less SQ_LIFT[the step's frame] (MK's feet height x 1.08: 27, 93,
  125, 119, 73, 70); out of it, the floor again if she was lifted by us (vars+63 bit per

  Checked: over a standing Shadow unhit, landing behind her (both sides, the theater and
  the Pit); a Shadow jumping in at four timings is hit (12); hit out of the air by
  Shadow's attacks she ends on the floor in her stance.

### Sonya's leg grab (2026-11-16)

- **done** MK CD: down + LP + LK + Block, close (mkmoves 'leggrab'); here down + X + A +
  Z. Her block (mkinputs.emit_leggrab_pad) turns those buttons, all held, into back + Y
  in her pad word: EC's own throw (behind) when she's close - its range, grab, victim
  and damage (20) - and its Y attack out of range. A flag per player (vars+64/65, kept
  LEG_KEEP calls, alive while she throws) picks leg_plan's poses: MK's bend, hands down,
  handstand (squeezed into EC's lift), the arch (MK's longest pose) held to the release,
  the way back in what's left; 6 free slots of their own (42:16 42:15 42:00 41:60 41:50
  41:4F). GENERIC_VARS 66.
- **found** Our drive runs every other frame; EC loads the thrower list (BLA.CO $1B10,
  at 0 6 12 16 24 28 32 frames) and 42:11 ($1A62/$1A68, ~46) itself, so the throw's
  poses showed for 2 frames between the leg grab's. emit_leggrab_drive writes the leg
  grab's poses due then into her module's list in RAM ($1B000/$21800 + $1B10, and the
  two immediate bytes) while the flag lives, and the throw's back when it's gone.
- Checked (leggrabcheck.py, both sides; in allchecks): out of range no throw; close the
  throw with her arch, no throw pose, 20 damage; then an ordinary throw with her throw's
  poses only. Prior attempt (Codex: the chord through move $0D) left her stuck: the
  move without EC's grab set-up.

### Raiden's daze: measured, not done (2026-11-16)

- mkdazed.py makes his 7-pose sway from the disc fine (MK's animation $B3-$B9). With
  it his art grows 151010 -> 159256 bytes and CHW.PL would be 193816 of MAX_PL 188416
  (player 2's slot), before his 5312-byte tail (scripts, fatality data). Room now:

  still be ~4.5 KB over. Needs room found first: his tail elsewhere, or art saved.

### Raiden's daze and Sonya's interrupted square wave (2026-11-16)

- Raiden's art now holds all seven MK daze frames ($B3-$B9), decoded by
  `mkdazed.py`. (Codex first drove them from his own block; superseded below:
  every generic fighter's daze is driven the same way.)
- Space: his art banks use a 42,000-byte estimated grouping threshold, which
  gives real banks of 28.6-31.8 KB; a bank's offsets are signed 16-bit, so
  scorpion_full asserts each bank <= 32 KB (nothing checked it before).
  Six near-identical existing Raiden poses share storage: each pair has the
  same anchor and differs by at most 200 pixels, asserted at build time. This
  keeps the whole seven-frame daze and both of Blade's original effect sheets
  within his player-two .PL limit. Sheet $45 and its impact tiles are unchanged.
- An earlier experiment removed sheet $45 or pointed it at projectile tiles.
  Removing it made MK fatalities and EC Vendettas on Raiden freeze; sharing it
  changed projectile impacts. Neither approach is used in the build.
- Sonya's light hit reaction during the square wave now descends from her
  actual airborne position at 12 pixels per update. A knockdown instead keeps
  EC's own airborne arc. Both land and recover; neither is a capture of MK's
  interrupted animation. `squarewavecheck.py` checks both hits, landing, and
  subsequent walking on both sides, in addition to ordinary flight.
- MK CD comparison: at several starting positions, square wave travel remains
  fixed until the screen boundary, and holding forward, back, or down during
  the flight does not steer it. The existing fixed-duration flight was kept.
- Direct checks: seven Raiden sway frames on both sides; Sonya's square wave,
  including interruption, on both sides; and Scorpion, Sonya, Sub-Zero and
  Raiden fatalities against Raiden on both sides. A Sonya mirror with both
  players using square wave also lands normally. A fresh build from the two
  discs matches the tested candidate in all 20 output files.

### Every MK fighter's daze sways (2026-11-16, later)

- Reviewing the Raiden daze: only Scorpion ever swayed. Sub-Zero, Johnny, Liu
  Kang, Kano and Sonya had their 7 sway poses in their art (`mkdazed.py`, commit
  0e63eac "wiring in progress") but nothing played them: EC's dizzy showed 41:25
  for 12 frames and 41:26 for 2, looped (two poses, a twitch). Raiden's new
  driver also flickered: EC's loop wrote its own pose between the driver's calls
  every ~8-14 frames (2-frame blips), as the leg grab's throw list did.
- Fix, every generic fighter: EC's dizzy script holds 41:25 (`mkinputs.dizzy_hold`,
  as Scorpion's spear_script did: now both steps show 41:25 and hold $7FFF - its
  first step still ends ~8 frames in, while the sway shows pose 0 anyway; the old
  hold of 120 gave Scorpion a 2-frame blip every ~4 s too, gone now). His sway
  is cycled by `daze_pad` at MK's timing (DAZE_SEQ), from the first pose each daze
  (the counter resets when he isn't dazed); not under a finisher, a Vendetta or
  a grab (as r_drag).
- Room (measured on the built disc, not estimated): Sonya's block had 4 bytes
  free, SUB.BIN 10, PLYR.CO's gap 64. So: each block makes a 4-byte bsr.w to
  `daze_pad` (76 bytes) in its module's padding between BLA.CO's end ($51A6) and
  the block ($5200): loaded with the module, never used. Its frame comes from
  `daze_tail` (8 bytes + the 36-byte table) once, after PLYR.CO's stubs. The
  per-player counter is chosen by bit 5 of the fighter's address (P1 $2E188 / P2
  $2E330). GENERIC_VARS back to 66.
- An EC winner gets no FINISH HIM (his jab knocks the MK loser down): FINISH HIM
  is the MK winner's block's.
- `dazecheck.py FIGHTER [--as-p2]`: a real FINISH HIM from a duel; all 7 poses,
  in MK's order, held 8-12 frames. On the disc before this fix: Raiden fails
  (holds of 2 frames, out of order), Sub-Zero fails (2 of 7 poses). allchecks
  runs it for all seven, both sides.

### Polish: square-wave interruption, shadow kick and Raiden's fatality

- MK CD reference captures: `shots/reference/mk_sonya_squarewave_hit.mp4`,
  `mk_johnny_shadowkick.mp4` and `mk_raiden_fatality.mp4` (local recordings,
  not distributed). A jumping kick or punch against Sonya in flight sends her
  into a tumbling knockdown. Convert an ordinary pending hit into EC knockdown
  only while her square-wave lift is active, then leave EC's knockdown in
  charge of the arc. This matches the kind of reaction, but its animation and
  trajectory are EC's, not a frame-exact reconstruction of MK. Checks exercise
  both attack strengths, landing and recovery from both sides.
- Johnny's flight uses the corresponding MK shadow-kick poses. The missing
  portion of the large trail is placed in a separately uploaded effect sheet;
  `shadowkickcheck.py --verify-art` decodes it and compares all 1,109 clipped
  pixels with the original. The effect tracks his position and facing and is
  removed on exit; both sides and mirror matches have a check. It shares sheet
  $77 with his green bolt and fatality heads, within its 91-tile allocation.
- Original MK Raiden fatality reference: four victim palette flashes, 3 frames
  on/3 off, at captured frames 180-200; explosion at 208. Add four blue palette
  flashes and EC's blood effect at the explosion, on either player side. The
  tint and six EC blood droplets are approximations, not MK's exact palette
  and spray. The test checks palette restoration and blood objects. His victory
  lightning overflow had already been handled by `fxl_pieces` (see the earlier
  Raiden lightning notes).
- Scorpion's ghost experiment: a separately uploaded static $7C sheet did spawn
  an object, but visual captures still showed fragments rather than a frozen
  fighter. The experiment was kept out of the build; spawning an object alone
  is not evidence of a correct afterimage. Further work needs to establish why
  the static sheet's tiles are not displayed correctly, and test mirror matches
  and VRAM use before it is safe to include.
- Johnny versus Xavier: see the earlier Contest investigation; the attempted
  fatality was issued before recovery, not proven to be a game bug. Independent
  human and CPU Xavier duels both completed the fatality in this round of tests.
- Sound decision: keep the deterministic disc-rendered effects. All nine are
  byte-identical to the current extracts (`mksound_render.py --check`). Sound
  effects captured from other fresh fights can differ in timing; the comparison
  does not establish byte identity with every possible emulator recording.
- Jab measurement in original MK CD: Scorpion's standing low punch (A) against
  idle Sub-Zero, with positions read from PRG RAM $7D758/$7D85E, connected at
  a 73-pixel anchor separation and missed at 75 (stationary during the punch).
  This is one fighter, opponent and attack; EC's hitbox distance has not yet
  been measured under the same conditions, so it does not establish whether
  the current conversion's reach is faithful for all fighters.
  On the candidate, the corresponding Scorpion-versus-Sub-Zero test with EC's
  X input hit at 97 EC coordinate units and missed at 105; `hitmatrix.py`'s
  approach-and-settle method sampled those spacings. These are each game's
  own coordinates and artwork is scaled, so comparing the raw numbers alone
  is not sufficient to decide on an adjustment. No reach change was made.
- Theater Overkill audit (P1 victim, Shadow winner, stage $1B): all seven MK
  fighters reached the drive-by and the $7FFF lying state. Visual contact sheets
  show Kano lying in an awkward curled pose and Johnny's final pose missing much
  of his upper body. Johnny's existing `knockdown` capture itself is clipped at
  the screen edge; its aliased `lying` frame $42:0C cannot supply the missing
  pixels. This needs a complete original MK lying frame decoded from the disc,
  and a review of Kano's pose. The other stages, the other victim side and the
  round's transition after the scene remain unaudited. The attempted end flag
  `$30AB7 & 3 == 3` did not become true for either Scorpion or the Blade control,
  even though both completed the drive-by; do not use that flag as proof of a
  freeze or of successful transition.

- Follow-up: Johnny's frame 131 and Kano's frame 144 were decoded complete from
  MK's OBJ and added as separate `lying` recipes, with fresh anchors, instead of
  reusing the clipped or curled knockdown captures. The Theater drive-by now
  shows their complete poses; both also reached the Pit's spikes as victims.
  `shadowkickcheck.py` still verifies Johnny's trail and both player sides.
  On the final candidate the 11 basic boot/gameplay scenarios passed; focused
  square-wave and Raiden-effect checks passed on both sides, Johnny's shadow
  kick and green bolt passed on both sides including mirrors, and Johnny and
  Kano fatalities passed normally and in player-2 mirrors. The reconstructed
  20-file disc matched a disc-only preparation/build byte for byte. The full
  parallel `allchecks.py` run was terminated before reporting; it must not be
  treated as a full-suite pass.

### Further afterimage and Raiden-space probes

- Scorpion's experimental independent $7C sheet is correctly encoded in the
  disc: decoding the sheet reproduces all 2,827 pixels of the intended MK
  stance, with zero mismatches. The spawned object remained at its original
  world x while Scorpion moved (for example x=399 while he moved to x=349),
  but its on-screen picture was still fragments. This narrows the problem to
  runtime sheet/tile upload, sprite attributes, or rendering, rather than the
  encoded pixels or the ghost following Scorpion. The experiment is not in
  the build. Its camera moved from 324 to 337 in this sample, so edge clipping
  must also be accounted for when testing the result.
- Raiden: the final candidate's CHW.PL is 187,074 / 188,416 bytes, leaving
  1,342. Rebuilding all six distinct poses at bank estimates 39,000–43,500
  gave art sizes 158,508–158,912 bytes versus 149,992 with all six shared;
  those estimates do not solve the space problem. Restoring just block pose 3
  at the existing 42,000 estimate grew the rebuilt art 1,316 bytes, leaving
  only 26 bytes in CHW.PL. At 42,500 it grew 1,290 bytes, leaving about 52.
  These are isolated packing probes, not tested changes; restoring just one
  is not a solution for all six, and this experiment did not alter the build.

- Finished (later the same day): a build from these sources matched the
  candidate in all 20 files; `play.py test` 11 of 11 and `allchecks.py` 95 of 95
  (it now also runs Johnny's shadow kick from both sides and in mirrors, and
  Raiden's flashes and blood from both sides). MK CD's square-wave reference
  shows Sonya, kicked by a jumping Sub-Zero, falling to the floor, lying, and
  getting up: the knockdown is MK's reaction. Clips: `shots/polish/`.

### The teleport's double; FINISH HER for Riptide (2026-11-16, evening)

- **done** Scorpion's teleport leaves MK's frozen double (idle pose 3) where he took
  off, until he lands (MK CD reference: shots/reference/mk_scorpion_teleport.mp4).
  Not a fighter frame (a second object showing one draws stripes: one unpack area per
  fighter) but an effect sheet uploaded whole at the fight's start: Blade's $44 chunks,
  kept in his .PL (scorpion_full strips only $45), renumbered $7C (no other file
  registers $7C), one frame (patches.scorpion_ghost_art); BST.CO's init loads $7C and
  sets $19C = $7C (scorpion-no-fx). The double copies his $1E (palette line) and $20
  (facing). Codex's experiment found the approach and the palette.
- **found** Codex's version froze every Scorpion fatality at its end: BST.CO's resource
  list (jsr $696C, inline $0044 $0045 $FFFF) was shortened to $007C $FFFF, leaving the
  old $FFFF behind; $696C returns past the list's own $FFFF, so the CPU ran the stray
  $FFFF as a line-F instruction. That code (module entry +$1A, $1B020) runs only when
  $6D28 (a finisher's end, PLYR $19472 -> $17D5C) re-inits the fighter: fights were
  fine, the fatality's end hung ($1CD4 never 3). Now the leftover word is a nop.
- **found/fixed** Scorpion's teleport variables (vars +70..+80) are shared by both
  players: in a mirror match the standing Scorpion's call cleared the teleporting one's
  "taken over" and "camera held" each frame, so his punch never landed (also on the
  last commit). Now he leaves them alone while his opponent is a Scorpion teleporting,
  and the double's flag is a bit per player (vars +81), each double its own task.
  teleportcheck.py: the double (shown, at the take-off x, his palette, gone after),
  --mirror, --verify-art (all 2,827 pixels of MK's pose); allchecks runs them.
- **fixed** FINISH HER: mkinputs.FEMALE lacked Riptide (id 14), so she heard FINISH HIM
  (and a man's scream in Scorpion's fatality; Johnny's split punch hit her). The
  FINISH voice now tests a 32-bit mask of the women's ids (14 bytes instead of 8 per
  woman: every MK module has it). Checked on the audio: FINISH HER on Riptide (Johnny,
  Scorpion, Sonya) and Jetta, FINISH HIM on Larcen.
- **seen** The select grid: 20 fighters (presses 0-19; 20+ wrap). Not on it: ids 11, 25,
  26 (the Contest's last opponents), 16 Thanatos and 22 the Senator (duelstate --unlock).
- **found (open)** Scorpion's Contest ending fails on this build (allchecks 96 of 97):
  the boss fights can stall. Seen: Eternal (id 26) hit in the air (his move $2B) by
  Scorpion's move $24 at 3 health -> health 0, a knockdown, then he stands at 0 for
  good: no KO, no FINISH HIM, the clock stopped ($AB7 & 3 = 2); in another build the
  Dark Champion vanished mid-fight. Not today's code: the last commit (e71680a) fails
  the same way once Scorpion's .PL is merely padded by 4 KB (the CD load timing shifts
  every later fight), and disabling the mirror check in RAM just before the hit
  changes nothing (Scorpion never teleported in that run). A latent softlock, to fix.

## Goro's Lair as Eternal's arena (2026-11-17)

- **Which fights use which arena** (Codex, Contest run): Eternal (id 26) fights on

  home stage (GAME $C28) is $12 or $11. SUB.BIN tests $12 itself in two places
  ($6FD0 the round clock, $12DF6 with $11: the boss rounds' end): stage-number rules,
  kept.
- **MK's Lair on the disc**: BACKGD6.BIN (the Pit is BACKGD4): six longs then RNC
  blocks - tiles (571), plane A map 128 x 32 (palette line 3), plane B map 128 x 25
  put 3 rows down (line 2), lines 3 and 2, the eyes' 5 tiles (open to shut). MK
  blinks 8 pairs of eyes in plane B (mklair_disc.EYES, from its name table over
  20 s); ours are open. tools/mklair_disc.py --check matches Codex's capture of
  MK's planes and palette exactly (HUD rows and eye frames aside).
- **MK's camera there**: x 0-480, starts at 160; plane B x = 40 + 3/4 camera x;
  the floor rows have a perspective scroll (line 200 at 1.19x), not copied.
- **patches.lair_stage**: built on the arena's own init (SUB $791A, kept): xmax
  640 -> 480, start x $144 -> 160, the five back bands' speed (x mode 4: x =
  (camera - $3C) * speed / 1024, SUB $E4CE) -> $300. 572 tiles (the arena had
  344). The arena's stage-finisher hooks ($3400A/$3400C) are both rts: it never
  had one. What mk-death-art took for its per-victim death table (EC.BGI $10EA)
