## Shang Tsung in place of the Eternal Champion (2026-11-17)

- **The Eternal Champion is two fighters and a show** (stock Contest, recorded):
  fighter 11 (CHA.CO + ECH.PL: white gi, purple glowing hands) fights first; at
  his KO his module waits on $1488 and sets $AB7 bit 7 (CHA $BE2); the arena's
  code (EC.BGI) then runs the spirit show (a purple wisp drifts round the room, the
  four windows change picture: $34758 picks their cell maps by fighter id $0B/$1A,
  the orb object $356CE flashes the palette, sets its $A0 = 1) and fighter 26
  (ECX.CO + ECX.PL: floats, fire hands, a smoke serpent) comes in; his entrance
  clears $AB6 bit 2 (ECX $2736). SUB's module table: SUB $4F04 (id 2 SHA.CO ... id
  26 ECX.CO), then the .PL list (ECH.PL for 11).
- **So the arena can't simply be swapped**: patches.lair_stage (Goro's Lair from
  MK's BACKGD6, which works in a duel) replaced EC.BGI and the show never ran -
  the fight against fighter 11 stood still for good (contest run: no KO, no 26).
  The Lair stays out of the build until its art goes in without losing the
  arena's code.
- **His colours**: palette line 2 (player 2's own), from a list of 16-word
  palettes in his module (CHA.CO $3CC8-$3DA8 and $4626-$579C, ECX.CO $3A32-$5622:
  his own, alternates, one per special whose entries 12-15 are its glow; entries
  9, 10 are EC's blood reds) plus CHA's glow fade steps ($35F0/$360A/$3624/$363E,
  entries 1-13, set by $69FC). Line 1 is the arena's (its windows: setting
  Shang's colours there speckled the windows and left him Eternal-coloured);
  its entries 7-9 cycle. First tried line 1: wrong.
- **Raw sheets**: a .PL also has raw-tile sheets: the sheet chunk (word +4 = 4,
  sheet * 4 at +$A) names a tile source (+$C); the source chunk (+4 = 0, its
  number * 4 at +$A) is $18 = packed (resource at +$E) or 4 = raw tiles (at
  +$E, byte count at +$12); a raw piece's attribute word's low 11 bits are its
  first tile. ECX's $9F (261 frames, 1766 tiles) is the second form's whole
  fighting moveset: missing it left him Eternal's poses in Shang's colours.
  The other raw sheets are effects (slashes, sparks, smoke, the windows'
  creatures $A7/$A8), kept.
- **plsheet.py** draws any .PL's every pose (game's unpacker + the sheet's piece
  lists). ECH.PL: sheets $9B (254 frames), $9C (113); ECX.PL: $9D (120), $9E
  (70), $A0 (113, mostly empty records). One bank section each (ECH $60C, ECX
  $2E8), as Blade's: the art region is the first chunk.
- **MK's Shang (EMP.OBJ/SCH)**: frame descriptor +6/+8 = x/y offset (feet at y
  108). Own poses: 1-6 stance sway, 7-11 skull throw, 12-18 the skull, 19-21
  arms raised (the morph), 22-45 morphs into the others, 46-50 knocked down to
  lying, 51-55 reeling, 56-60 smoke rings. No punch, kick, crouch or jump art:
  MK1's Shang only throws skulls and morphs. Colours: PAC.PRG $1C110 (Goro's too;
  not yet checked against MK's own Shang fight).
- **patches.shang_tsung / tools/mkshang.py**: each EC pose sorted by kind (outline
  against the stance, plus hand labels per sheet range: mkshang.OVERRIDES) and
  given that kind's Shang poses in order along a run of frames; the fire
  projectiles of form 2 his skull, its smoke serpent his smoke; collision boxes
  kept (EC's reach and body); $9F's tiles laid one pose after another (15 poses,
  1146 tiles). Colours fitted to entries 1-8 and 11 of line 2. Names: HUD
  (over SUB's 'ETERNAL\0CRISPY\0': SUB.BIN is full), versus, stats, win field
  ('     SHANG').
- **fixed** mk-death-art took a run of tile numbers in EC.BGI's window map
  ($35072, drawn by $34824) for a per-victim death table and rewrote a few
  tiles of that window. EC.BGI is skipped now.
- **Shang's behaviour (the maintainer: "he's jumping around... not shooting the fireballs
  correctly")**: the CPU's choices pass PLYR $15114-$15120 (and $152D4-$152E0):
  $1628 = directions ($1D22: 4 away, 8 toward), $1626 = button index ($1D1E),
  $1634 = special ($1D20). The Eternal Champion closes in with move 8, a hop
  (script header vy $FFEA, gravity $1D000): that was the jumping. His chains
  (patches.shang_chain: CHA.CO $3E0C, ECX.CO $3B76, 28 scripts each) also have
  walks ($A, $B). Now: move 8's script is the walk's steps (patches.shang_tsung),
  and the AI stub (mkinputs 'ai', on stage $12 only) drops up/down, clears the
  button and turns any attack into the ranged special (id 11: $2D, 26: $2C).
  Room in the PLYR stub area came from sharing the finish and drag stubs.
- **Their ranged specials are EC's, not MK's**: id 11's $2D spins a ring of
  sparkle orbs round him (effect objects, descriptor CHA $383E/$3952 = his own
  raw sheets $A1/$A3, the orbs $A2) which then fly; id 26's $2C uses the arena's
  orbs ($B8/$B9: DEC.P1, DECWDW.BIN), its $2D turns him into a flying fireball
  (his frames 9D:64-73, trail sheet $A4). Redrawing $A3 as Shang's skull grew it
  97 -> 162 tiles and the Contest froze in that fight: an effect sheet keeps
  its tile count. A real MK skull throw needs our own projectile (as the MK
  fighters', mkinputs.emit_proj).
- **Colours 12-15 of his line flash during his cast** (white blotches when
  Shang used them): he keeps 1-8 and 11. Poses are scaled as the MK fighters'
  (scorpion_full.scale_pose in MK's palette, then fitted).

- **Status (2026-11-17, the maintainer's call)**: the Eternal Champion is back in the
  build. Shang drawn over EC's boss looked and fought like EC's boss (the maintainer:
  orbs everywhere and colours that looked wrong, the
  opponent knocked about in mid-air by the orbs). Next: Shang as his own fighter,
  as the seven MK fighters were made (his MK moves and a real flaming-skull
  throw, his colours checked against MK's own Shang fight), using the boss slot
  only for the Contest's hand-off to the Dark Champion and the ending. Morphing
  after that. The experiment: branch shang-skin-experiment (0aa7d3f).
- **found** Shang's colours: PAC.PRG $1C130 (dark robe with gold trim, natural
  skin, white hair), not $1C110, which is Goro's (Goro is right only under
  $1C110). The experiment used $1C110: the "terrible colours". Order after the
  seven fighters' ($1C030-$1C0F0): $1C110 Goro, $1C130 Shang.

## MK's own Shang Tsung fight, as the reference (2026-11-17)

- **found** MK CD's fighter picks: sub-CPU PRG-RAM words $7BAC4 (player 1) and
  $7BAC6 (player 2): 0 Johnny, 1 Kano, 2 Raiden, 3 Liu Kang, 4 Scorpion,
  5 Sub-Zero, 6 Sonya, 7 Goro, 8 Shang Tsung. Setting $7BAC6 to 8 once the game
  has picked (1 player: the ladder's first opponent; 2 players: player 2's
  choice) and before the load gives a real Shang fight in the Courtyard: the
  health bar says SHANG TSUNG, the CPU drives him (1 player) or player 2 does.
  $8F50/$8F52 only cache which fighter's art is loaded (the loader skips a
  fighter already there; ids 8 and 9 take their own load paths): setting those
  loads nothing. 9 hangs on "Loading". The fighter tables at PRG $33232 (pointers)
  are the select screen's ladder lists, not read at fight time.
- **checked** his colours live: player 2's line holds PAC.PRG $1C130 exactly
  (the VDP's 9-bit form of the same words).
- tools/mkshangref.py makes the states (states/mk_shang_1p.state, _2p) and labels
  every frame of five scenarios with EMP.OBJ frame numbers (exact pixel matches:
  MK draws his frames unscaled) -> extracted/mk_shang_ref.json.
- **seen** (frame numbers are EMP's; durations in game frames):
  - stance: 1, held (no breathing loop).
  - walk toward: 2 (3), then 3 held while he glides, ~1.75 px/frame.
  - walk away: 4 (5), 5 (3), then 6 held, ~3 px/frame. The CPU backs off from a
    jump-in this way, and walks back after a morph.
  - skull volley: 7 (2), 8 (2), 9 (5), 10 (4), then 11 held with the arm out while
    three skulls form at his hand (12-16) and fly (17) one after another, ~40 frames
    apart, ~8 px/frame; a skull's burst on the opponent is 56-59 (so 56-60 are
    the skull's explosion, not morph smoke). Then the CPU morphs (28, 29, 30 into
    Raiden; 43, 44, 45 into the gold form) or throws again; the morph back plays
    them in reverse.
  - hit (Kano's jab or high kick): 53 (5), 54 (3), 55 (5), pushed ~13 px back.
  - knockdown (uppercut): 46 (9), up off the top of the screen, lands 48-50 against
    the edge, back to 1 ~80 frames after the hit.
  - The CPU's Shang in his own form never walked in to attack: he stands at range,
    throws volleys and morphs. Player 2 driving him is not a faithful source
    (after a button he cycled 53-55 for good: MK never meant him to be played).

  fighter 11 ($142A = 9) on stage $12; if won ($14C5A), one against the Dark

- **checked** (worktree experiment, patch blade-boss-test): plain Blade's files as

### Shang's skull volley and his CPU (2026-11-17, afternoon; branch shang-fighter)

- **done** His cast, move $34, is his own script (mkinputs.skull_plan; Blade's $34 is
  BLA.CO $4D74, 41 steps): 7, 8, 9, 10 at MK's timing on 40:60-40:63, then 11 on
  Blade's release frame 42:2E for 65 ticks (~130 frames). While 11 shows, emit_skull
  spawns three skulls 5, 25 and 45 calls in (MK: 10, 51, 91 frames). Each skull is an
  object at his feet: MK's skull frames are placed from his feet, so frame 13 then 16
  (4 and 5 calls) grow in his hand, 17 flies 13 px a call (~6.5 px/frame), and on a hit
  it stops on 56 then 57 (MK's burst; 58-60 don't fit). Sheet $AC (no EC file uses it),
  5 frames packed to their non-empty tiles: exactly the effect source's 91.
- **found** EC's projectile hit engine (PLYR $19D64) tests only the owner's $122. Each
  skull's task registers ($6A38, which also gives it his palette bits) but puts the old
  $122 back; it takes $122 when it starts to fly and clears it when it bursts. Without
  that, a bursting skull kept hitting: knockdown, then dizzy.
- **found** The projectile's $A4 overrides the hurt-box reaction ($118: box 1 $1B, 2 $1A,
  3 $1C). The hit level ($79, PLYR $1A2CC, 16-byte entries) sets the stagger: level 2
  (Blade's, the MK bolts') ~40-70 frames, level 7 ~26. Skulls use 7: three separate
  staggers ~46 frames apart, as in MK, also with Kano in the corner.
- **found** EC's stun meter: the victim's $14C adds each hit's damage and drains 5
  about every 120 frames; reaching $14E (40, then +5 each time) knocks him down and he
  gets up dizzy ($1F). A standing player eats 4 skulls quickly enough to trip it. It is
  EC's rule for every fighter in the mashup; left as it is.
- **found** The CPU's $1628 holds the pad's directions (1 up, 2 down, 4 left, 8 right),
  not away/toward (the earlier note had it from player 1's side).
- **done** CPU (r_ai, Shang only): no jump or crouch; while his opponent's feet are 16+ px
  up he holds away from them and doesn't throw; on the ground never away (EC's block) and
  toward only from beyond 150 px; any special is his volley; punches and kicks are dropped.
  Measured, Kano idle for 30 s: volleys began at frames 196, 590, 1520, 1744 (a round
  ended between the 2nd and 3rd), i.e. ~40-240 frames of standing between volleys; MK
  stands ~245 frames between volleys in his own form, then morphs.
- **found** Blade's walks are moves $A (back, BLA.CO $3FE4) and $B (forward, $4040): 6
  steps of 40:1B..40:16 / 40:16..40:1B. The walk drive corrects EC's frame only every
  other frame, so EC's own walk frame showed 2 of every 6 frames; with one pose for both
  directions on those slots, the glide flickered. Shang's $A/$B are those scripts on
  40:65 / 40:64 (mkinputs.glide_scripts).
- r_ai is now a table walked by a loop: a compare block per fighter outgrew Sonya's code
  block (12 bytes over) once Shang joined.
- Side by side with MK's CPU Shang (volley, jump-in): superseded by the video below.
- **closed (below)** His backing off was Blade's walk speed and EC's jump reached him,
  so the jump kick landed (blocked) where MK's falls short. No morphs yet.

### Shang's glide at MK's speeds, its start poses, a check (2026-11-17, evening; branch shang-fighter)

- **found** MK's world x for player 2 is word $7BE26 in MK's PRG-RAM (screen x moves
  with the camera). Gliding toward: 2,2,2,2,2,2,2,1 px a frame = 1.875, from the start
  pose on. Away (the CPU backing off a jump-in): ~2 on 4, ~3 on 5, 3.25-3.5 on 6 (41 px in
  12 frames, lag frames included). Player 2 can't glide away in MK: away starts his cast.
- **found** A walk's speed is its script header's first long (PLYR $180FC; 16.16, 2 units
  a px a frame). With the first step's flag $0020 EC reads the pad: the speed as is when
  moving the way he faces, halved against it ($18126). Blade: forward 4, back 8 -> 2 px a
  frame both ways. Shang: forward $3C000 (1.875), back $D8000 (3.375).
- **found** EC ends a walk after its last step (bit 7 of $BE, $182F4) and starts it over
  from step 0 while the direction is held; steps with flag $2000 / $4000 make a loop
  (saved at $181C6, taken at $182AC while $15E counts). Shang's walks are his start
  poses, then one step of 4096 ticks on the held pose: no restart, no repeated start.
- **done** His start poses (MK: toward 2 for 3 frames; away 4 for 5, 5 for 3) are in
  walk_fwd's art (EMP 1-6) on 40:66, 40:67, 40:68 (glide.json, mkinputs.glide_frames),
  2 ticks each. His block has no walk drive (the scripts show his poses).
- **found** Step flag $0020 is EC's "may block": a fighter on such a step, holding away
  from an attack within 192 px ($C0), is put into his block (PLYR $19960 for strikes,
  $1A0D8 for projectiles); it also lets the CPU's block choice run ($1972C) and lets him
  be thrown ($1E08A). At MK's speed Shang still stopped in that block as Kano's kick
  started, and the kick reached him there (his $10C got a hit's stun, no damage).
  MK blocks with a button: his held away pose goes without $0020
  (mkinputs.GLIDE_NO_BLOCK). He still stops, turns or attacks the frame the pad
  changes. Measured: the gap stays 116-128 px through the kick, as in MK.
- **known difference** While he holds that pose he can't be thrown and the CPU doesn't
  choose to block.
- **check** tools/checks/shangcheck.py (in allchecks): glide poses and px both ways, a clean
  volley (3 hits of 10, 40 frames apart, no knockdown), the CPU's jump-in (the kick
  never touches him, he backs off). With Blade's speeds it fails (60/54 px wrong, the
  kick touched him).
- Side by side with sound (MK CD left, ours right; played twice, MK's sound then ours):
  shots/shang/shang_glide_skull_vs_mk.mp4 - glide toward, volley, the CPU's jump-in.

### Shang's morph into his opponent (2026-11-17, night; branch shang-morph)

- **found** EC's own mid-fight swap (Eternal 11 -> 26, on e0375ae): after the final KO the
  screen freezes 189 frames (~3.2 s) while ECX loads, then a ~15 s spirit show. MK CD's Shang
  freezes too: ~150 frames in three stills during the smoke into Raiden, 47 on the way back;
  he stays Raiden ~280 frames under SHANG TSUNG's bar.
- **found** Memory: player 2's .PL loads at $51800, player 1's at $81800 (Word RAM); the
  sheet table is $30EF4 + 4 x sheet; a fighter frame is unpacked from his sheet into his own
  VRAM area, so any slot draws any loaded fighter's sheets. MK fighters' files are 155-187 KB
  of art + ~26 KB of module: no room to preload one, so he morphs into the fighter already
  loaded - his opponent.
- **found** A mirror match's two modules are byte-identical but for 2 counters: the module is
  position independent. Kano's and Shang's modules (both Blade's) differ only in sheet bytes,
  scripts (+$3DE2-$51xx) and the MK block: a running task survives a swap.
- **found** Fighter set-up (PLYR $190C6): $12BBC uploads the colours at $11E (the alternate set
  at +$20 when both fighters' ids are equal), $1A278 the colours again and the move table
  ($A6: P1 $31646, P2 $31742, 63 entries) from the module's script list after its colours.
  A script's header is 20 bytes; $AA = its steps, $AE = the current step, $C4 steps - 1,
  $C2 the step. The move task's TCB is (($88)); +$26 its step's ticks left.
- **found** Blend art: EMP 22-42 are MK's blends, 3 per fighter: Johnny 22-24, Kano 25-27,
  Raiden 28-30, Liu Kang 31-33, Scorpion 34-36, Sub-Zero 37-39, Sonya 40-42 (43-45 Goro).
  Pinned on 40:38-40:4C (no script, code or throw table uses them); his tile region still has
  room (52922 of 120112 bytes).
- **done** mkinputs.emit_morph / emit_morph_subs (his block), r_ai: after a volley, standing,
  no skull out, both at 24+ health, an MK opponent: his opponent's three blends (5 calls each,
  his stance step held, the CPU kept still), then his module saved in his .PL's tail
  (+$25A50, $6800 bytes; patches.mk_code), the opponent's module copied into his slot, his id
  the opponent's, $12BBC + $1A278, his script remapped. While morphed he is that fighter: full
  moveset, MK block, AI filter (only id 11 is limited). The copy's drag entry jumps (+$67F0)
  to his saved controller: after 150 calls, standing still 5 calls with no projectile, the
  saved module goes back, the blends play backwards. A knockout while morphed swaps him back
  at once (same move, same step). Vars at module +$67E0 (CHA.CO padded to $6800; every MK
  block now ends below $67E0).
- **found** Two full blocks (Scorpion's) are a mirror match: the dispatcher skips the second.
  The copy's full flag is cleared so his trampoline is called; it goes on to nothing (player
  1's block already did Scorpion's part for both).
- **checked** vs all seven MK fighters: morphs and returns repeatedly, their moves (jumps,
  attacks, specials), no freeze in 4000 frames; vs Shadow, Blade, Blast: no morph. KO while
  morphed, either way: Shang at once, the round ends, the next starts as Shang.
- tools/checks/shangcheck.py: + a morph cycle vs Kano (blends 40:3B-3D in and back, id 13, Kano's
  moves); the volley and jump-in run after it.
- Side by side with MK CD's (into Raiden, its freezes): shots/shang/shang_morph_vs_mk.mp4.
- **found** (the maintainer saw it: the morphed fighters looked terrible) EC's palette: $12C3C only
  writes the buffer at $1502(a6); $702E hands it to the palette tasks ($D09A), which take a
  target (-$6956(a6)) to the output sent to CRAM (-$69D6) through per-colour fade levels
  (-$68D6) - ~20 frames, so his new art showed in the old colours (speckled). -$67D4 = -1
  sends the whole output (D258). His line goes into target and output at once, levels 0.
- **found** On screen, a frame set in $1A shows two frames later, new colours one frame later
  (images vs RAM/CRAM). So he goes blank (40:4D, an empty record: patches.mk_code) a call
  before his colours change, both ways, and the CPU gives no input meanwhile (r_ai: a mark,
  $A55A, at MORPH_MV in the morphed copy) - else a move's first frame showed over the blank.
  He is gone ~5 frames as he swaps; no frame shows art in the other fighter's colours
  (checked, all seven, two cycles each; shangcheck models the delay and failed the old build
  at exactly the two bad frames).
- **known difference** Only into the opponent (MK: any fighter, with a CD freeze). Morphed, he
  wears the fighter's mirror-match colours, as EC's mirror match does (darker; Scorpion and
  Sub-Zero swap colours, MK1's way); his sounds while morphed: not checked. Against EC fighters
  he doesn't morph.
- **found** (allchecks: Johnny's Contest ending froze once the morph shifted the fight's
  timing) EC builds a move table ($1A278) without clearing it: the Dark Champion kept
  Shang's $2E/$31/$34/$35/$36. In the Contest his AI isn't his own (no fighter 26 between:
  with Shang in slot 11 he comes straight after; in a plain duel he never chose $35 in
  20000 frames) and it chose $35: Shang's leftover pointer ran into his code (the sub CPU
  trapped at $5EE); with the table cleared, an empty entry ran from address 0 (he vanished:
  frame $0000, y garbage). **done** patch move-table-clear (PLYR $190C6/$190F4: the first,
  redundant colour upload makes way for clearing the table) and r_ai drops any special the
  fighter has no move for. The open "boss fights can stall / the Dark Champion vanished"
  note above (Scorpion's ending, 2026-11-16) may be this same cause: not checked.

### EC's throw step loop (2026-11-18; branch throws)

- **found** The grab ($1CA06 in a module at $1B000: BLA.CO +$1A06) puts the victim at the
  thrower's x, his record list in the thrower's $9A (throw_table entry for the victim's
  id - 2) and the thrower list (BLA.CO +$1B10: frame, control word per step) in $15A.
- Each step (PLYR $19124): the thrower shows the list's frame; control word bit 15 swaps
  sides (both turn: bchg bit 3 of $20/$100 on both; d5 = 2 x (victim x - thrower x),
  negated if the victim's bit was clear), bit 14 negates d5 unless the record's flags have
  bit 11, bit 13 frees the camera, the rest are eor'd into the thrower's $100/$20. The
  victim gets frame -> $9A, dx - d5 -> $9E, dy -> $A0, flags eor'd into his $100; the step
  lasts the record's ticks (2 frames each). The camera clamp can shift both fighters.
- The victim's state $37 ($17EB8): $1A = $9A; x += $9E if his $100 bit 11 is set, else
  -$9E (so a record's flag $0800 also turns his dx round); y += $A0; $20 bits 11-12 (his
  sprite's flips) from $100. Step 0 thus places him dx ahead of the thrower.
- tools/throwsim.py simulates it from a module's lists (matches the measured positions).
- **found** (the maintainer: "he's not facing the other way at the end like MK"; "bent over in the
  wrong direction") All MK throws measured (tools: scratch rec of states/mk_*_2p): the thrower
  follows through facing forward and turns to his victim once it's over (Kano ~44, Raiden ~64,
  Johnny ~40, Liu Kang ~60; roll throws Scorpion/Sub-Zero ~68-72, Sonya ~88; her leg grab
  ~108). Roll throws land the victim ~100-115 px behind (~48-52), tosses 108-187 px (~52-60);
  the leg grab holds him bent over her 16-44, flings him ~175 px, lands ~104.
- **found** EC's side swap (thrower list control bit 15, step 3) turned the thrower mid-throw.
  throw_plan's "peak" (longest-held pose) was Kano's turned stance (MK's capture poses 23-28:
  his stance mirrored) - he stood upright mid-throw.
- **done (Kano)** No swap mid-throw; the swap on the last step (he turns as MK's does, ~48);
  his MK throw poses (16-22, to the mirrored stance) at MK's timing up to THROW_TURN_EARLY
  before the swap, then his stance; each thrower-list step loads the frame his animation
  shows then (mkinputs.throw_list: EC's own loads had flashed a crouch, turned). Checked
  frame by frame: no wrong-way pose. Larcen lands ~180 px back at ~52.
- **done (Scorpion, Sub-Zero, Raiden, Johnny, Liu Kang, Kano)** Tracks from MK's recordings
  (tools/dev/throwcmp.py track); swap mode (Kano, Johnny: he turns on the last step) or stance
  mode (the others: no swap, EC turns him as he stands; the two pauses after the release
  stretched to MK's turn, mkinputs.throw_post).
- **fixed** (sheets, every 4 frames) The victim sank up to 75 px into the floor at the
  landing in every throw. EC turns a frame upside down about its anchor (measured: art at
  y..y2 is drawn at 1-y2..1-y, screen y = anchor - 13 + y), so an upside-down frame hangs
  below the place a record puts him; MK's heights are the sprite box's bottom.
  throwsim.solve now raises each step by how far its frame (flipped or not, from the .PL's
  piece list: throwsim.bottoms) reaches below the anchor. Heights match MK's within ~2 px.
- **fixed (landing)** The victim lay down ~14 frames after the release (MK's: ~2-4 after
  touching down). (1) The caller cleared the knockdown's vertical speed ($CA) every airborne
  frame, so he floated down 0.5 px a frame in the flight pose: now set to THROW_DROP (3 px)
  down instead. (2) The last (floor) step was held until MK's landing, a step after ours:
  now THROW_LAND_TICKS (1 tick), so the release follows the touchdown. Lying ~7 frames after
  the release; Kano, Johnny, Raiden within ~4 frames of MK.
- **tried** Cutting the knockdown before EC's own landing (phase 3 at once): EC still plays
  its first-landing replay later and he floats ~30 px up, lying. The cut has to stay on the
  bounce. THROW_DROP 6: the landing check misses and the bounce isn't cut.
- **note** After any build, re-save states/menu.state (tools/play.py test --no-build) before
  recording: duelstate.py starts from it, and it holds the last build's SUB.BIN in RAM (a
  recording after a build alone ran the old code).
- **open** Roll throws (Scorpion, Sub-Zero): MK's victim lies flat on touching down (~50),
  ours ~58; Scorpion stands out of his kick ~62 in MK, ~70 ours. Sonya's normal throw (her
  block is 26 bytes short) and her leg grab's track.

### The Dark Champion hang: our effect objects deleted as palette tasks (2026-11-19; branch objdelete)

- **found** (reptile branch, Johnny's Contest ending; layout-sensitive: SEN.BG padded alone
  hung it) The sub CPU's BIOS restarted (PC $5EE: its wait-for-VBlank loop; the maintainer calls at
  $5F28.. reset to rts, EC's program gone). tools/cpuregs.py reads both CPUs' registers from
  the core (its m68k_get_reg / s68k_get_reg, found with nm). Traps (bra * poked into each
  BIOS exception slot $5F40..) caught an address error at frame 26083: the CPU ran into
  data at ~$53920, in DEC.PL (the Dark Champion's art, loaded at $51800) - from EC's pose
  unpacker ($11784: data bytes pick jumps through a table), fed bytes of his pose that
  something had changed (+4 / +$14 on alternate bytes at $54C82; $52A6C..$52A71).
- **found** The writer, with a write watch (Python handlers put into the core's s68k
  memory map, every block mirroring PRG RAM): $FE70, EC's sprite priority list insert
  (next +$A, prev +$E, head -$1E92(a6)) through $752A6D - the insert stopped at node
  $2D5C2, whose prev was $0000E16E (should be $0002E16E): from there $E16E (SUB.BIN code)
  was taken as a node and its "next" ($4E752A5F, code bytes) written through.
- **found** Node $2D5C2's history (contestcheck's on_frame probe arming the watch): made at
  25574 as an object by Johnny (player 1); at 25608 freed by our code with jsr $6A14 - which
  is $D068, the PALETTE TASK free (unlinks +0/+4, pushes it onto the palette pool
  -$6CB8(a6)), not an object delete; at 25633 the Dark Champion's palette fade ($69F0 ->
  $CF4E, $D07E) got it from that pool and set its +$C = 1, +$E = 8 (its countdown); $D210
  counted +$E down to 0 - the high word of the node's prev, still in the sprite list.
  So every Johnny shadow kick (mkinputs.emit_johnny_shadow: jk_drive) and every Sonya kiss
  landing (emit_kiss: gq_hide) put a live object on the palette pool; what that did depended
  on which fade got it and when (hence file layout). In the playable build too.
- **fixed** Both now delete as EC does everywhere (dozens of `bset #2,$82(aN)`): the flag;
  EC's object loop ($105B0 -> $10972) sends it message 6, unlinks it from both lists, frees
  it to the object pool and frees its task. Same size (6 bytes for 6).
- **found / fixed** That delete then crashed as player 2 (line A at $1EB52): message 6 goes
  to the object's handler $84, which szf_head_desc copied from Blade's projectile descriptor
  (BLA.CO $3B26 +26: $0001EB48, absolute - right only in BLA.CO loaded for player 1; EC's
  .OC files are the same modules linked for player 2's address). In player 1's module it was
  whatever code lies at +$3B48 of the host (harmless by luck). The head, kiss and shadow
  objects need no messages: their descriptor's handler is now an rts in SUB.BIN ($FB10,
  which never moves). (MK projectiles and skulls already set their own $84.)
- **check** shadowkickcheck also requires that no object is on the palette pool's free stack
  ($29348) and that the sprite list's links agree; the old build fails it on both sides.
  The old open note "Sonya's kiss as player 2: sub CPU crashes (PC $5EE)" fits this too.

  move $34 and the slide on $35.

  155584 of 188416 bytes (~32 KB for art). tools/checks/reptilecheck.py (allchecks) guards his
  pit bottom, freeze and slide.

  box on 42:2E stretched to 127 px: scorpion_full.spear_reach) - measure its bytes in ECX.PL;

### Play feel: the maintainer's play test (2026-11-19; branch reptile-spear)

- **fixed (music)** The Pit is the Senator's stage mode ($1F): its CD track is EC 13 (SUB.BIN
  $ABEC ids / $AC12 tracks), the Senator's theme; MK's Pit music (MK 4) was still on EC 17
  (Blast's, from when the Pit was there). build.STAGE_MUSIC = {13: 4}.
- **fixed (jumps)** EC has one jump animation for every direction (Kano: 40:02, 40:06, then
  41:00-41:04 = 12+12+10+8+2 frames in the air, the same up, forward and back; Blade never
  flips), the 'jump_up' group; its first poses were the capture's stance (the recordings start
  standing). scorpion_full.stance_lead skips them. MK's forward/back jumps roll: mkinputs
  jump_plan / emit_jump_drive - in the step-loader hook (r_jumpup; the PLYR stub at $1AEDC
  puts the script step's frame in $1A, then calls the block: a write from the input task was
  overwritten every ~1.5 frames), while in the air in move 8 with an x speed ($C6), $1A = the
  capture's airborne poses (<= 10, free slots) on MK's timeline fitted to EC's 22 calls, by the
  sub CPU's frame counter ($2800E); back jumps by image through the same poses. Airborne =
  feet (anchors.json) above the first pose's: the captures' stance after the landing is
  numbered anew. Kano, Johnny, Liu Kang, Sub-Zero (tables in his .PL tail), Reptile, Scorpion
  (his full block). Not Raiden, Sonya: no block room (BLA.CO fills the slot to $51A6; room
  needs Blade code they never run removed, proven unused).
- **fixed (glide)** Blade's B is a hopping lunge (slide steps at the header's speed, +24 px and
  10 up on the hit): MK fighters glided ~60 px. MK's attacks end where they started (MK's
  $7BE34 = player 1's x): mkscripts drops Blade's movement (hit flags/data kept).
- **found (timing)** EC adds ~12 frames around every move's script. MK (Kano, mid range):
  punches hit at 10 and cycle every ~20-24 frames; ours 9 / 33-35. mkscripts PUNCHES give
  those frames back from recovery and the strike hold (cycle 28); Kano's headbutt 50 -> 38
  (MK 39). Kicks left (MK's low kick cycles at 49, ours 40). MK's player 2 x: $7BE3C (screen),
  $7BE26 (world); MK's contact gap ~81, ours ~50.
- **fixed (round 2)** The CPU Reptile's timed specials, forced during round 2's intro, held it
  before FIGHT: the clock stood at 99 and his AI never ran. They wait for the clock to leave 99.
- **fixed (reproducible)** The roll reads the jump captures' order.txt, not in data/mk_meta
  (never read before): buildreads + mkcapmeta --out added them; a fresh clone then identical.

### The maintainer's hang: found and fixed (2026-11-19, night; branch reptile-spear)

- **fixed** (traced on hang-wip's notes) Kano knocked down ($1C) by Reptile's throw was still
  lying when the CPU's Reptile started a spear: the pull code (rs_drive, and Scorpion's r_drag
  the same) took any hit state $1A-$1D as 'reeling' and counted the throw's wind-up frames as
  'before the hold: pull' - it dazed him lying; EC's landing code (PLYR $189D0) then looped in
  his task, its thud ($3EE) flooding the unbounded sound queue, the main CPU stuck on the Z80's
  bus, the sub CPU on its frame command: the picture stopped. Now the pull acts only on the
  frames after the rope's hold (mkinputs.emit_spear_phase: 40:5E, 40:5F, 42:32-42:35).
  Reproduced (Kano jumping every 70 frames) at 1438; with the fix none in 12000. reptilecheck
  (spear pulls to 36 px, daze 81 frames) passes.
- **seen, unconfirmed** With both healths poked every frame through time-over (a forced draw),
  the sub CPU took an address error drawing Reptile (frame 42:44, beyond Blade's 64-frame sheet:
  the second form's own pose?) and, against Shadow and on the playable build, the emulator itself
  segfaulted - poking memory (serialize/unserialize) through the round's end is suspect. A
  real time-over (Reptile ahead on health) ends and round 2 starts. Open: a draw at time-over
  played honestly (no pokes).

  sleeps. Its frame slots hold MK attack poses for our fighters ("a seizure"). EC's sleep
  (SUB $1042C) pops the return address into the task block and runs the next task on a shared
  stack: neither registers nor the stack survive a sleep. EC starts the round clock when both
  intros end (no intro: the clock ran during BEGIN CONTEST).

- **fixed (a sound stuck playing over and over)** The maintainer: 'after a while I hit them and the hit sound
  is stuck playing over and over'. EC's PCM driver (SUB $48706, each frame) reads every channel's
  address ($FF0021/$FF0023) and keys the channel off when it equals its entry's end address (+$16 in
  the loaded sound table: FIGHT's at PRG $48E2A). A bank's loader fills +$14/+$16 only for the entry
  that loads a clip; an entry naming a clip an earlier entry took keeps 0. mk-hit-sounds pointed
  $3EC and $3EF at one SMACK1: $3EC's end was 0, so its channel reached SMACK1's $FF, looped to
  address 0 (the loop start the driver set from +$16) and played sound RAM's first clip again and
  again until another sound took the channel (traced: 1806 frames on, 14 wraps in 91). Now every
  sound-table entry has its own clip (FIGHT: SMACK1 twice, 50 of 63 pages; the 3-line voice banks
  CHA/CHI/OWL: a 16-byte silence for each repeated id - the driver plays an id's first entry).
  soundbankcheck fails a table naming a clip twice; the old disc showed 7 such entries.
- **fixed (heads cut off in knockdowns)** The maintainer: Sub-Zero's head disappears when knocked down,
  Johnny's too. MK's knockdown and sweep captures (recipes) were taken with the victim flying into
  the left edge of MK's screen (and off its top): the recipe draws each MK frame where MK drew it,
  clipped by the screen, so the tumble (Sub-Zero's frame 135, 17 px off: up to 407 pixels gone) and
  the lying frames lost their heads and chests (the sprite list was fine: 23 sprites, his pose 5
  pieces). tools/capture/mkunclip.py moves each cut pose onto the screen with its anchor (anchors.json:
  where the build places it), so it shows where it did, whole: 21 captures (every MK fighter's
  knockdown and swept, some jump_back / hit poses). Fatality and spear captures keep their framing.
  allchecks runs mkunclip --check. Checked (sprite layer, Scorpion's uppercut mid-screen): Sub-Zero's
  and Johnny's tumble show head, mask and arms.
- **seen** EC fighters hitting each other draw no blood (as EC): the maintainer doesn't want it added.
- **done (jump landings)** MK's FEETLAND ($3F1, FIGHT_NEW: its own clip, 55 of 63 pages) as an MK
  fighter lands a jump (emit_jump_drive: move 8 or $28 reaching the floor while rolling); any
  other move on the floor ends the roll silently (a jump attack's landing, a knockdown - FALL2 -,
  a hit). Room: the drive's pose tables are a byte a call (an index) and the distinct poses (words)
  - 132 bytes were a word a call; Kano's drive 284 of 284. tools/checks/feetcheck.py (allchecks): once a
  landing forward / up / back, never walking, all 7.
- **fixed (straight-up A / B showed no kick)** The maintainer: Kano jumps straight up, kicks, does
  nothing. EC's A / B straight up load 41:06 / 40:03, mkinputs.JUMP_UP swaps them to 41:37 / 41:36
  (jumpup_c poses 4 / 3 - Scorpion's numbers). The other MK fighters aren't exact: a group is
  spread in list order, and 41:36 / 41:37 come first: his rising poses, no kick. Now (spread)
  slots naming one pose take its last slot's place: 41:36 = 41:15's, 41:37 = 40:24's (C's knee and
  kick). Checked Kano: A kicks, B knee - kick - knee, C as before.
- **seen** Riptide's bubble (her move $2F, QCF + Y; not $34, her charge down-up + Z throw, which
  tints nothing) tints its victim for ~60 frames: his palette line cycles every frame (line 3 for
  player 1). On an EC fighter (Blade) it reads as an even watery blue-grey; on an MK fighter (Kano)
  as red and blue blotches - the tint goes by palette index (EC's fighters share one layout of
  shades; MK's palettes don't). The cycling isn't written into the sub CPU's palette copy
  (-$6956(a6): one write in 80 frames, $CF46's whole-palette load): the main CPU does it.
  **open** a tint that reads as water on MK palettes. **checked** Riptide's reach on MK fighters is EC's (measured: her farthest hits on Kano / Sub-Zero within

- **fixed (a freeze: no free effect object)** Johnny's Contest froze in match 8 (sub CPU at $102,
  illegal). Traced: EC's object loop pushes each object's link, runs its tasks; the link of the
  pool's first object ($2CABC) read $E188D32A - PLYR's hit spark ($1987C: jsr $6954, then
  move.l a3,$8C(a0) unchecked) had got a0 = $2CA2E: EC's allocator (SUB $10844) leaves a0 on the
  free objects' stack (-$35D2(a6)) with carry set when none is free, and none of EC's ~80 spawns
  checks the carry (every .CO; PLYR's dust at $18D50 the same). The pool (35 objects of $A6 after
  the stack, SUB $10504) was full: 6 of our blood drops + 5 EC sparks + 5 EC dust clouds.
  patches.ec_object_pool: 35 are built as before (the objects stay where they were: checks look at
  $2CABC + n x $A6), then the free count is one less - the first object ($2CABC) is a scratch one,
  never listed; the allocator (rewritten in place: its clear is a loop now, $10850 still the clear -
  bsr'd from $107F2 / $107FE) hands it out, cleared, carry set, when none is free; its $88 'task'
  points into its own tail ($9C), so a spawn that sets a task writes inside it too. tools/checks/poolcheck.py
  (allchecks): the free count held at 0, jabs both ways - alive, the list whole, the free stack only
  pool objects, the spawns' writes in the scratch object (the build before: the list broke in 5
  frames). (Tried first: blood leaving 10 objects free - it took Raiden's fatality blood; and
  shrinking the pool to 34 - every object moved 4 bytes and the checks' object scans missed them.)
  (Why now: the punch-hold build moved the timing; any build could have hit it.)

  fighter, all the same (build/scratch/slotview.py). scorpion_full.pick sent the 'knockdown' action
  to knockdown_pose before the exact-pin check: every pin got 'the first pose back down'. Now pins
  are poses. Their art: 13 different were 7 KB over Scorpion's .PL; the knockdown takes 2 air poses
  (one of them EC's flight pose, 42:18 - shared art) and 3 on the floor, the flattest always (spread
  evenly it was passed over on all but Kano - Sub-Zero's 28); Scorpion 4 in all (KD_POSES_CAP),
  Kano's floor just his lying 23 (KD_FLOOR_N: his .PL has no room; it was what he showed before).
  Seen (labels a frame behind - $1C follows $1A): Sub-Zero hit, flying, legs up, flat, on all fours,
  standing; Sonya, Scorpion the same; Kano flies and lies on his back. The sweep's fall was right
  already (no special pick for 'swept').
- **fixed (a freeze: a fatality started on a loser fighting on)** Kano's Contest froze in match 4
  (Larcen): the sub CPU ran into Word RAM ($B7E40, tile data). Traced (kfrz: exception traps in the
  Contest, a step trace with the frame wait released by hand - poke -$7FF6(a6) - and the load's
  caller by its return addresses): EC's frame unpacker ($117D6) read Larcen's frame from garbage -
  the first $B800 bytes of his .PL ($51800) were ZLR.BIN, his death art, loaded by Kano's fatality
  (gk_load, from Larcen's move task). Larcen was out of his daze (a hit ends it: he fights on at 1
  health) and had knocked Kano down; Kano's input (back taps once crossed over, + punch) went through
  anyway. gkok (second victory room, from gk_idle): the fatality starts only while he isn't thrown,
  hit or knocked down, and the loser is dazed or being hit (by his last press). The knockdown change
  only moved the timing (main passed this Contest; the race was there).
- **fixed (kicked: sprawled, then a ball on the floor)** The maintainer: 'bad animation when MK people get
  hit', 'they still roll like a ball when they're kneeling'. Every MK fighter's hit_kick capture is
  a kick that knocks him down: doubled over (2 poses), off his feet in a tuck, flat, hunched on the
  floor. mkinputs.kick_plan took all of it for EC's kick reaction (he stays up): Sub-Zero doubled
  over, sprawled on the floor, then sat hunched in a ball. Now the reaction stops at the first pose
  off the ground (feet more than 14 px up from his idle's) or lying (wider than 1.5 x tall), then
  plays those poses back the other way: doubled over, deeper, up again (Kano: his one pose before
  his feet leave - 26 px up by his second). Crouched hits ($1E) stand him into the punch reaction:
  no ball there.

### Knocked out, lying flat (2026-11-21)

- **found** (the maintainer: Trident knocked Sub-Zero down in round 2, he didn't lie flat, and the stage's
  vines came up round him) A round-ending knockdown or a stage Overkill on an MK fighter: MK's
  knockdown drive (emit_kd_drive) played his landing, flat pose and then MK's getting up (legs up,
  on all fours) while EC held him down; then EC's own frames: 42:17 (EC's landing - his half-up,
  curled landing pose) for ~28 frames, then 42:0C lying. Seen frame by frame: Sub-Zero vs Trident on
  the theater ($1B, Larcen's: the drive-by Overkill) and on Trident's own stage ($18: a plain KO).
- **fixed** The drive holds a fighter out of the round - no health ($76 <= 0) or a finisher on
  ($1CD4) - on the flat pose: mkinputs.KD_FLAT (kd_plan: the floor table's first call on its
  flattest pose, the knockdown's and the sweep's). And 42:17 is drawn lying
  (scorpion_full.knockdown_pose: the landing as lying): every MK fighter's knockdown is the drive's
  now, so EC shows 42:17 only holding a beaten fighter down and as a throw lands him. Seen: the
  theater Overkill - flight, flat on landing, flat to the end; the plain KO - the same.
- Trident's stage (TRI.BGI $3400A): Overkill when the loser is at x $308-$340 (facing one way) or
  $108-$140 (the other), then knocked down ($159 bit 6); x $50-$A8 its Sudden Death. Not
  reproduced here (the duel's poked positions didn't hold).

## Going public, and the maintainer's play reports (2026-11-22)

### Daytime: fixes from play, the 32X in the standard disc, the repo made public-ready

- **Reptile's versus portrait**: EC has portraits for ids 2-25 only and drew id 26 as 11 (Shang's
  now). Id 26 draws Sub-Zero's tiles with a green palette of its own, in bytes freed by folding EC's
  four copies of its palette loader into one (patches.reptile_portrait; versuscheck.py).
- **Straight-up jumps as long in the air as MK's**: a slower launch with a lower gravity per fighter
  (mkinputs.jump_up_launch, header +8/+12); forward jumps keep EC's gravity (jumpcheck.py).
- **The 32X support in the standard disc**: build.py builds the plain disc, then adds the 32X
  (build32x.add_32x); `--no-32x` for the plain one.
- **Spear**: the victim held 22 frames before the yank (EC's hit stun frees him ~32 frames after
  the hit; MK's 35 needs a new hold).
- **Sonya's ring and square-wave inputs**: forward held + HP punched as her square wave (the
  generic throw rewrite read toward + HP as EC's back + HP); fixed (sonyainputcheck.py).
- **Liu Kang after his fireball** looked like he walked: his stance steps' timing (STANCE_STEPS).
- **Trident's Sudden Death froze on MK losers**: its per-loser tables ($1A34, $1B82) have
  entries for ids 2-25; each MK fighter gets Blade's, sheet-shifted (Shang's data moved for 26).
- **Slash's dinosaur / pterodactyl in stripes, Dawson's stage in stripes**: MK modules unloaded
  graphic $45 (Blade's blast) when a stage had reused its VRAM; MK fighters no longer load or free
  it (mk_own_graphics). tools/dev/stageaudit.py checks every stage with every MK loser against
  Shadow (single-frame map flickers only, Shadow's too).
- **Frozen or dazed fighters blocked** (the maintainer: white sparks on a frozen Dawson): the block
  decision now sends a dazed fighter to the hit path (dazed_no_block).
- **Choppy sound after pausing the play window**: 100 ms of silence queued after a pause or a
  stall (play.py).
- **Public-ready**: MIT licence (LICENSE, SPDX tags, licensecheck.py); the layout restructured
  (tools/ = the commands, tools/lib/ the library); docs rewritten; Codex's 32X outputs deleted, his
  Cinekill note kept (docs/research/cinekill_film_research.md).
- **History** (the maintainer's choice): main is two commits - the repo's start (2026-11-13 14:47) and
  its state at 17:33 today - as alphafox02; the full private history is kept outside the repo. From
  here every change is its own commit, with its real time.

### Evening

- **Riptide's bubble turned Kano red and blue** (19:51): EC fades a fighter caught by an effect to
  one of six tint palettes in his module before his own (BLA.CO $3D20-$3DDF, reached through the
  module header +$18 by PLYR's tint tasks $1AC08; RAX, Slash, Trident, Xavier, Chin Wo and Decker
  use them too). MK fighters had Blade's, made for Blade's art's slots. Each MK fighter's are now
  his own colours mapped by brightness onto each tint (patches.tint_palette; tintcheck.py). EC keeps

- **Sonya's sweep** (20:30): EC's launch comes from the victim's own knockdown header (PLYR $18984
  loads $C6/$CA), so it can't be data; r_swept_by needs ~80 bytes and her block has none (104 over
  as a room_put subroutine; her rooms full; SUB.BIN and PLYR's gap full). Parked; the room found so
  far is in mk_fidelity_spec 5. sweepcheck.py checks the other six (Larcen slides 9-12 px; hers 135).
- **Blast's Vendetta on MK losers** (still switched off): allowed in RAM, as player 1 on Kano and
  Liu Kang it plays as on Blade - his own colours, then EC's burnt remains (BST.VC $6CA, a fixed

- **Liu Kang's flying kick was slow getting going** (21:45): live on MK CD (the scroll taken out:

- **Reptile's fight showed the Pit's bridge for a second** (the maintainer): the Pit's stage task runs

- **Sonya's room**: every MK fighter's module is still Blade's code under MK's art (walk, jump,

  Only the victory loops, the pre-fight taunt and the blast sheet were reclaimed. Next: find Blade's

### Night (an unattended run)

- **Pit bridge** merged (139/139, identical). **Docs** for newcomers merged with it.
- **Blade's dead script bodies as code room** (mkinputs.GEN_DEAD, room_put's fourth room): every MK
  module keeps Blade's chain of move scripts (patches.CHAIN_START $3FA0-$51A4, 53 scripts); PLYR
  $1A278 builds the move table from them at fight start, reading only each one's slot and step
  count, and the block writes its own scripts into 15 of those slots every frame (r_generic: 'move.l
  script,4*id(a0)'), so those 15 bodies (~1 KB) are never read. (patches.mk_attack_scripts, which would
  have appended MK scripts to the chain, is called nowhere.) Measured before using them: a fighter's
  running script ($AE) over 24,000 frames of random MK-vs-MK fights was in Blade's other scripts
  ~3,500 times a fight, never in those bodies; deadscriptcheck.py repeats it. Only the basic attacks'
  slots count (scripts the build edits - the dash, the spear - stay out). Renumbering (roster) runs
  before mkcode writes there.
- **Sonya's sweep, MK's** at last: r_swept_by as a subroutine in a dead body ($422C), its bsr the last
  4 bytes of her block (5600 of 5600). Larcen drops 9 px (135 before); seen frame by frame.
- **Kano's heart held up**: his timeline (CAP_FATALS 'kano') ran to capture frame 320 and ended on
  pose 8 shown as 7 - his empty fist (the nearest kept pose): the heart showed under a second. MK's
  beats on (poses 8-12, 8 frames each) past its capture: now to 472, 8 and 10 shown as 9 and 11 (the
  heart in each); he holds the heart ~320 frames (mkfatalitycheck: at least 180, both sides). A false
  alarm on the way: against Shadow the check reused a duel state saved on an older disc, with the old
  table already in RAM (the stale-state guard looks at file positions only; the gate deletes them).
- **32X study** (read-only): docs/research/32x_opportunities.md.
- **The title screen's logo is a still picture in GAME.DAT** (found tonight, after the 32X study ranked
  'the logo by the Genesis' first): the menu screen (build/states/menu.state) is plane B, a still
  picture (0.6% of its pixels change in 4 s - the emblem; the water doesn't move), 40 x 28 cells, every
  one a tile from 709-1537, and those tiles are GAME.DAT's own, uncompressed, in two runs: $3B0C-$718C
  (709-1144) and $71AC-$A2AC (1146-1537). The red emblem and yin-yang above it are 16 sprites (lines 0,
  1, 3). So the logo can say ETERNAL KOMBAT by redrawing tiles in GAME.DAT - a data patch from the
  discs, every player, no video and no 32X. Open: the lettering itself (EC's logo font has no K or B;
  MK CD's own KOMBAT lettering, or letters built from EC's), shared tiles (829 tiles for 1120 cells),
  the plane's map (not found as plain rows), whether the screen's palette (lines 0-2) has room.
  **MK CD's title** (MIDWAY / MORTAL KOMBAT in MK's gold, on red; build/states/mk_title.state, made
  from MK CD's attract by pressing Start) is plane A, 774 tiles, unpacked from **RJC.PRG's RNC block
  at $A868** (24,736 bytes; mkpitbottom.rnc1, as the portraits'). So 'ETERNAL' (EC's, GAME.DAT) and
  'KOMBAT' (MK's gold, RJC.PRG) can both be made from the player's discs by a script. The colours:
  EC's picture fills lines 0-2 and line 3 is the emblem's and the text's; MK's gold would be matched to
  the screen's colours or one line reshuffled. The maintainer chooses the look.
  The plane's map (entries like $255A: palette lines 1-2, tiles 709+) is in no file as plain words,
  rows of 40 or 64, or tile numbers less 709/708: packed, or made by code. Not needed: the build can
  rewrite tile pixels in GAME.DAT with the cell -> tile layout kept as numbers in data/ (as the pose
  recipes are), measured once from build/states/menu.state. Mock-ups: build/shots/title_mockups.png
  (rough: the subtitle patched over with water).
- **Fade-ins**: every stage fades both fighters in from grey (EC's, the jungle too); in Reptile's
  fight only he is at full colour from the start (his palette set directly) - the earlier note's
  'player 1 grey on the Pit' is EC's ordinary fade.
- **Sonya's normal throw, MK's** (the last room she needed): MK CD's measured with throwcmp (track:
  [((6, 0), 10), ((-5, 12), 2), ((-38, 72), 2), ((-29, 72), 2), ((-58, 21), 2), ((-79, 13), 2),
  ((-95, 0), 2)], ticks 10, 2 x 6, turning as she stands at MK frame 87); her leg grab's hit-drive
  tables to a room (room=True) freed 60 bytes of her block for the throw's code. Side by side with
