## The title says ETERNAL KOMBAT (2026-11-23)

### Morning (with the maintainer)

- **The title logo, the maintainer's look.** First mock-ups put MK's gold KOMBAT over CHAMPIONS and the
  subtitle in a box; the maintainer: it sat half over the graphics, and CHALLENGE FROM THE DARK SIDE could
  stay. Then MK's gold, shrunk to CHAMPIONS' size (loses its shine at 40%); the maintainer: black, like
  ETERNAL, would be fine. So KOMBAT is EC's own lettering: K from DARK, O and M from CHAMPIONS, T and
  A from ETERNAL, B from CHAMPIONS' P with its bowl repeated below; each letter only its own strokes
  (connected pieces of 12+ pixels: the first cut carried bits of neighbours - the maintainer saw a stray
  between A and T). The maintainer asked for it closer in with a little red: the K sits where CHAMPIONS'
  H was (x 194) in the emblem's glow.
- **What the picture allows.** Every cell's tile is its own among those that change (76, none
  mirrored); a cell has one palette line: the glow's cells are line 0 (reds), the letters' line 2,
  some line 1. Line 0 stops in steps (EC hid them behind its C and H); the glow now fades on into
  the K with 4 colours in line 2's slots 12-15, which nothing on the title or menu draws with
  (checked: plane A, sprites and plane B, title and every main-menu item). The menu keeps the title's
  palette (GAME.CO $39A, one loader at $186), cycling only line 2's colour 11 (the letters' gold
  shadow - KOMBAT's too).
- **Corrected:** the picture's tiles are one run, 709-1537 at GAME.DAT $3B0C (1145 is at $718C, not
  a gap); a few cells (above the logo) are mirrored. The picture made from GAME.DAT + GAME.CO + the
  measured map equals the screen exactly.
- Built: tools/lib/ectitle.py (patch title-logo), data/ec_title/plane_b.json (the map, measured by
  ectitle.py --measure), titlecheck.py (title and main menu, index for index). README's known issue
  about the title removed (it was wrong anyway: the logo was never the video).

### Late morning: the menus' music, and the 32X build made optional

- **The maintainer: left on the title, after EC's song it played random MK lines** (Scorpion wins, Raiden
  wins ...). Not the sound driver (a logging hook on its entry, $47FBA, saw nothing): CD audio. EC
  plays the menus' track 19 with the BIOS's repeat (MSCPLAYR, SUB.BIN $60DE; the track kept at
  $60FC), and cdaudio.add_mk_jingle appends the fight cues to that track, so the CD ran on past EC's
  song into them (FINISH HIM's music, the fatality's, the Pit's falls and announcer). The stock disc
  loops EC's song: its track 19 ends the disc.
- **The maintainer's idea: after EC's song, MK CD's Mortal Monday music** (its intro video's, from the ARENA
  logo: MK track 20, found by matching MK's boot audio against every track), then the cues as before.
  The title and main menu hand over to EC's intro video at 5:00, inside MK's song (measured: EC's song,
  then ~3 min of MK's), so they never reach the cues (menumusiccheck.py). **Open:** a menu screen with
  no such timeout (Controller Setup, Access Info) left ~5:40 still plays on into them.
- **Tried and taken out: a restart at the end of MK's song** (a check in EC's frame loop, SUB.BIN
  $BDCA / $BF22, playing track 19 again past the song's end, armed only once the menus' music itself
  was seen playing: unarmed, a FINISH HIM cue's position, still reported as the versus screen asked for
  the menus' music, set it off). It worked (Controller Setup: EC's, MK's, EC's again), but SUB.BIN has
  no room: full to PLYR.CO bar 2 bytes. Looked at and why not: $B880's 118 zeros are the versus screen's
  name-banner map ($B734 reads 3 x 40 words from it - an earlier check missed the reference, comparing
  '0xb880' with the disassembler's '$b880'); $D448 a runtime buffer (non-zero in 13 of 14 states);
  $1255E a palette save area; the gap after PLYR.CO 14 bytes; moving the throw landing routine (236
  bytes) into the fighters' blocks: Scorpion's block and rooms can't take it; moving mkrow: the shared
  blood routine calls it. Next: GAME.CO (the menus' module) hosting the check behind a ~24-byte SUB.BIN
  stub, once 22 bytes are found.
- **Found on the way: on the 32X disc every CD cue started half a second late.** fatality_music
  writes the cues' disc times for the plain disc's layout; the 32X steps then grow GAME.CO, which
  moves the audio tracks 40 sectors on. build.py --32x now writes them again after the 32X steps
  (patches.cd_times); checked exact on both discs (every MK fighter's cues, the Pit's, the menus'
  end).
- **The 32X support is out of the standard build** (the maintainer: off for the public until it does more):
  `tools/build.py --32x` adds it. The gate builds it beside the standard disc (build/Eternal Kombat
  32X) and runs x32check there.
- **The gate found a hang in the Contest's last fight** (Raiden's ending: the Dark Champion never
  finished). An MK throw's knockout: the throw landing (mkinputs emit_throwfix) cuts the victim's bounce
  and sends her knockdown script to the step before its lying step (emit_throwcall). The Dark Champion's
  knockdown is laid out otherwise - its 'fly till landing' is its first step, then his bounce, no lying
  step - so he was left to his bounce, which ended in the air with his motion gone, and EC's landing code
  waited for the floor forever. Now each MK fighter's block sends him to that first step every call
  while he's down: thrown, he flies, lands, gets up and falls to his next form, as after a hit. It showed
  only when a run's timing gave Raiden a throw for the last blow (a run nudged by one frame hung the same
  way, nothing else changed). contestcheck.py --throws (in allchecks for every MK fighter): next to him,
  throw him - all seven reach their endings.

### Afternoon: MK's back + kick inputs, and an audit of the basic inputs

- **done: back + B is the roundhouse, back + A the sweep** (MK CD: away + high kick, away + low kick;
  the manual's "spinning moves"). Until now only C and down + C gave them; back + B was a plain high
  kick and back + A a low kick. Humans only; C and down + C still work.
  - Where: EC's stand loop goes to the walk code whenever a direction is held (BLA.CO $1766), so a
    request made with back held is always read by the walk loop ($18B4). That read now calls
    patches.back_kick_code (60 bytes in each MK module's low room, $168-$1A4, after zf): CPU ($12A)
    or no away held - on to zfw as before; B (5) becomes C (6) and goes on as any request (so up close
    it is the close roundhouse, $25); A (4) starts move $13 at the standing attack's start ($1794),
    past the 'close' moves ($1F + request).
  - Why the sweep starts as a standing attack: EC's crouched attack ($19AA) never looks at the special
    request again, the standing one does after its first yield ($17B6). Sub-Zero's slide (back + A + B)
    comes out today with A or B up to 2 frames early through that check; started the crouched way the
    sweep swallowed it. Measured before and after: together, A 1-2 frames early, B 1-2 frames early
    slide; 3 frames early don't (the same before).
  - Room: the low room after zf was room_put's third room (zpose and tables of Johnny, Sub-Zero, Liu
    Kang, Kano, Sonya: 26-54 bytes). Those go to the dead script bodies now (room_put: victory rooms,
    dead bodies, then the block). Scorpion's block stays 5584 of 5600; a per-fighter routine did not
    fit his (36 bytes over), which is why the code sits at one fixed place in every module.
  - Checked (tools/checks/backkickcheck.py, in allchecks): all seven, as player 1 and as player 2
    (facing left): back + B = 6, back + A = $13, pressed together or after walking back; C, down + C,
    A, B, toward + A / B as before; back + B held then back released gives one roundhouse; up close
    back + A drops Shadow as down + C does. Sub-Zero's slide in the six timings above. The CPU: a
    2400-frame fight against a CPU Sub-Zero is frame for frame the same with the walk read poked back
    to zfw in memory (and that poke takes a human's roundhouse away, so it does what it says).
- **measured: MK CD's basic inputs against ours** (MK disc, 2-player states, poses matched to the
  captures in extracted/mk_moves*; ours: move ids in duels with Shadow, all seven the same).
  MK CD's pad: A low punch, X high punch, B or Y Block, C low kick, Z high kick, Start also Block
  (no pause in a fight; a 3-button pad: A low punch, B low kick, C high kick, Start Block).
  - The same in both: both punches and both kicks standing; down + either punch the uppercut; down +
    either kick the crouched kick; away + low kick the sweep, away + high kick the roundhouse (now);
    Block standing and crouched; jump punches (one for both buttons) and jump kicks (one for both;
    straight up its own); Block in the air nothing.
  - Different, left as they are:
    - the throw: MK CD's is high kick up close (no direction needed; toward + high punch there is the
      close punch). Ours: toward + Y (high punch) up close; B up close is the close high kick ($24).
    - up close MK CD's punches are another move (a head blow / elbow; Kano's headbutt, which we have)
      and its low kick a knee (not Sonya's). Ours play the far punch and low kick with EC's close hits.
    - Johnny's split punch: MK CD's is down + high kick (the crouch_z capture). Ours is down + Z, so
      Johnny has no crouched Block on Z, and his down + B is the crouched kick.
    - C alone is a roundhouse here; MK CD has no button for it. X / Y / Z / A / B are EC's order, not
      MK CD's (A X B/Y C Z).
    - a press made in the same frame as down: MK CD plays the standing move (not Johnny's high kick);
      ours the crouched one at once (mk-block-button's crouch code, deliberate).
  - Specials on MK CD take either punch where one is named (Johnny's bolt, Liu Kang's fireball, the
    ice blast, the spear, Scorpion's teleport, Sonya's square wave: LP and HP both worked); ours take
    the one in mkinputs.GENERIC_MOVES. Sonya's rings on MK CD: high punch, back, high punch throws
    them (and HP, back, LP); LP, back, LP and back, back, LP only jab. Ours: back + HP. Kano's knife
    and cannonball need Block held on MK CD (without it nothing); our cannonball is forward, down,
    back without Block.
  Fatalities on MK CD (mkfataltrial.py, the fatality music heard): Sub-Zero's F, D, F, Johnny's F, F, F,
    Raiden's F, B, B, B and Kano's B, B each with LP and with HP; Scorpion's Up, Up only with Block held;
    Sonya's F, F, B, B + Block. Ours take one punch each (mkfatalitycheck.INPUTS: HP for Sub-Zero,
    Johnny and Raiden, LP for Kano). Liu Kang's was not heard this time with 6-frame steps (D, B, U, F,
    D; F, D, B, U, F with and without Block) - the earlier find stands untested here.
- **allchecks: 151 of 151 passed** on this build (data track SHA-1 69646c45...).
- **not done: MK CD's whole button layout for MK fighters** (asked for during this work). What stands in
  the way, measured:
  - one writer makes the pad words (SUB.BIN $C4A4, each frame, from the main CPU's $8010 / $8018),
    through Controller Setup's tables in RAM ($1DD2/$1DE2 player 1, $1DF2/$1E02 player 2; written at
    boot and by the setup screen only). SUB.BIN has no room for a translation there, nor has PLYR's gap.
  - a routine at the start of the input task (where zblock runs) needs about 80 bytes a module:
    Scorpion's block has 16 free and no dead bodies checked; and the input task runs every other
    frame while the pad word is rewritten every frame, so the other readers of the pad (zpose in the
    step loader) would see the untranslated word on the frames between.
  - swapping the tables themselves would need the player's own setup put back when the fight ends on
    every path out of it.
  - a 3-button pad's Start is a toggle in EC (SUB.BIN $C4F2: each press flips A/B/C between kicks and
    punches), and Start pauses the game (measured with 6-button pads; the 3-button path keeps the
    Start bit too); on MK CD Start is Block on both pads and nothing pauses a fight. Block on Start
    and a pause on Start can't both be kept.
  - every check drives MK fighters with today's buttons.

### Evening: MK CD's inputs for a player's MK fighters

Asked for: a player's MK fighters do what MK CD does on the buttons they have (X LP, Y HP, Z Block, A LK,
B HK, C the extra roundhouse), the CPU and EC's fighters untouched. MK CD measured again where the
afternoon's notes or the manual left a doubt (2-player states, the pad driven frame by frame; a move told
by player 2's health, player 1's sprite box, or the picture).

- **found first: a fight's course turns on the sub CPU's timing.** A routine in place of the stand loop's
  request read that did nothing but call the old one and return (the same d0, the same flags) changed a
  2400-frame fight against a CPU Sub-Zero at frame 420 (his x 2 px off, then everything); with 4, 8 or 16
  nops more in it the fight was the old one again. So "gated on $12A" is not "the CPU unchanged": every
  instruction added where the CPU's fighter passes can shift a fight. The afternoon's back + kick routine
  sat at the walk loop's read, which the CPU passes (its check passed by luck of the count). **The rule
  now: new input code runs only where a human's fighter runs.**
  - EC's own code has such places: BLA.CO $1784 (jsr $6ADE, after 'tst.w $12A(a5) / bne' at $177E: a
    human's request from the stand or walk loop, just before it starts), $19B0 (addi.w #$D,d1: a
    human's crouched request becoming its move), and the recogniser PLYR $159D0, which returns at once
    for the CPU's fighter - so the type $28 code (mkinputs.r_taps) only ever sees a human's presses.
  - patches.controls_code holds the routines, the same bytes at the same place in every MK module but
    Shang's: the body of Blade's close-punch script (move $20, $47C8-$485C, 148 bytes; mkinputs.CTRL_ROOM),
    dead since each block puts its own script in that slot (deadscriptcheck.py, Scorpion's module now
    checked too; Shang's scripts are Blade's own, so his module has none of this - only the CPU plays him).
  - back + kick moved there (h, called from $1784); the walk loop's read is zfw's again, as on main. The
    low room after zf ($168-$1A8, 64 bytes) is free again but in Sonya's module (below).
  - **proof, per check:** the routine in the CPU fighter's module turned into a branch to itself in memory -
    reached, he would hang - and a 2400-frame fight is frame for frame the same; on a human's fighter the
    same trap stops him. **And across builds:** main's disc (6371989, built beside this one) against this
    branch's, Shadow under the same scripted pad against the CPU's Scorpion, Raiden, Johnny, Sub-Zero,
    Liu Kang, Kano, Sonya, and EC's Larcen and Blade: 3000 frames each, both fighters' move, x, y, health
    and sprite frame the same on every frame.
- **1. the throw: B up close** (kickthrowcheck.py). MK CD throws with the high kick next to the opponent,
  with nothing held, toward held, and away held too (measured: away + high kick up close is the throw,
  not the roundhouse). h: B within EC's own throw test ($17E0-$17FE's: sys $6AE4 within 8, his two
  flags) starts EC's throw with 'behind' chosen ($A4 1, past PLYR $17112's read of the held direction:
  with nothing held EC's side depended on which way he faced). Frame for frame the toward + Y throw
  (positions, frames, health, all seven on both sides). Toward + Y kept: it is EC's own and nothing on
  MK CD stands in its place (there it is a punch). Further off B, back + B and C are as they were.
- **2. Johnny's split punch: down + B** (splitpunchcheck.py), his down + Z a crouched Block like the
  others'. jat at $19B0 in his module only: B becomes EC's Z (move $10), Z itself goes to the crouched
  loop's 'no request'. The CPU's Johnny still reads EC's way. The split punch does nothing to a woman
  (ours as MK's: the check hits Larcen).
- **3. either punch** (eitherpunchcheck.py; mkinputs.PUNCH). Measured this time too: Raiden's lightning
  with LP and with HP. Not either kick: Johnny's shadow kick only with the low kick, Liu Kang's flying
  kick only with the high kick, Sub-Zero's slide needs both kicks (with Block instead of one: nothing).
  So the spear, Scorpion's teleport, the lightning, the bolt, the ice blast, the fireball and the square
  wave take X or Y (a list word with both bits), the kick specials as before. The fatalities of Sub-Zero,
  Johnny, Raiden and Kano take either punch (emit_gkbutton; mkfatalitycheck.py --other-punch, which
  allchecks runs for player 2). Reptile and Shang (the CPU's) as they were.
  - collisions looked at: down held, toward, then a punch with down still held is the special on MK CD
    too (Raiden: the lightning, not the uppercut), and was here already for the punch each special had;
    X, Y, away / toward held + X / Y and down + X / Y still give the punches and uppercuts; Sub-Zero's
    toward, down, toward + punch at FINISH HIM is the fatality with X and with Y (its last two taps are
    the ice blast's).
- **4. Sonya's rings: high punch, back, a punch** (sonyainputcheck.py, now both sides). Measured again:
  HP, back, HP and HP, back, LP and HP, back + HP throw them; back + HP alone, LP first, or the three 10
  frames apart only punch. r_taps' first step can be a button (BTN1 | buttons). The first press is her
  jab, here as there - and EC drops a special recognised during a move before the move ends (the rings
  came out only if the last punch fell on the jab's last frames): patches.rings_code, in her module's low
  room ($168, 62 of 64 bytes), called from the human-only part of her zblock, sends her move task to
  Blade's special dispatcher with the queued rings at once (as Kano's queued knife). Back + HP alone,
  which threw them until now, is a punch.
- **5. Kano's cannonball: toward, down, away, up - Block or no Block** (kanoballcheck.py). **Not as
  asked** ("require Block held"): the disc says otherwise. The four taps in that order do it with Block
  held and without (4, 6, 8 and 12 frames a tap, with gaps of 3, rolled through the diagonals); toward,
  down, away (what we had), down, away, up, the other rotations and directions tried, or the taps 16 frames
  apart: nothing. The afternoon's "without Block nothing" was the three-tap motion. His knife does need Block
  (away, toward without it: he walks), as it already did here. r_taps takes a fourth tap (DIR3 | third |
  fourth << TAP4).
- **6. Liu Kang's fatality: down, away, up, toward, down, no button - what we have.** mkfataltrial.py,
  40 candidates far and 40 close (10 motions x 4 / 8 frames a tap x Block or not): only that one starts
  MK's fatality music, with and without Block, at both speeds; the four-tap circles (toward, down, away,
  up and the rest), the afternoon's toward, down, away, up, toward, and away, down, toward, up, away
  don't. The afternoon's miss was the trial, not the input. Nothing changed.
- **also measured, left alone:** Sonya's leg grab comes out on MK CD without Block too (down + LP + LK);
  ours wants Z with it. MK CD's close punches (a head blow) and close low kick (a knee) need art we
  don't have.
- **room.** r_taps is one copy per block and the first block found runs it for both fighters, so every
  block grew by the same 46 bytes; each block's move list and entries went to its rooms (room_put) to
  pay for it. Blocks now, of 5600: Scorpion 5598, Raiden 5576, Sonya 5576, Sub-Zero 5544, Johnny 5302,
  Kano 5018, Liu Kang 4908. CTRL_ROOM: 120 of 148. Scorpion's block and rooms are full (2, 4, 12 and 2
  bytes): the next thing in his block has to bring its own room.
- **the gate found: Johnny's Contest run never threw the Dark Champion** (contestcheck.py --throws: 0
  seconds thrown, the fight and ending fine). Not the throw: the run pressed toward + Y within 70 px and
  kept him on 3 health, so the punch that press gives from a step too far knocked his forms out first,
  each time in this run (B's throws earlier in the ladder had shifted it; the other six still got a
  throw in). The run now keeps him on 12 while it throws, where a throw (20) knocks him out and a
  punch or kick doesn't, and only walks up and throws: all seven knock every form out by throws (12-16
  seconds of the fight thrown, was 1 or more), to their endings.
- **allchecks: 155 of 155 passed** on this build (data track SHA-1 c2625bc3...; 151 and kickthrowcheck,
  splitpunchcheck, eitherpunchcheck, kanoballcheck).

## MK's close moves; Sonya's leg grab without Block (2026-11-24)

Asked for: what was left of MK CD's inputs - the close punch and knee (new animation? room?), Sonya's
leg grab without Block.

- **MK CD up close (captured, mkmoves close_punch / close_knee: walk in, one press).** Low and high punch
  play the same move, its own 3 poses: a lean, a wind-up, the strike held ~22 frames, back the same way.
  The low kick is a knee, 3 poses (Sonya's are her far low kick's poses, drawn ~100 px further along).
  Kano's close punch is the headbutt the build already had. Scorpion's close punch poses are the ones
  the old throw_a capture shows, Johnny's nutpunch3's (neither read by the build before).
- **in the build:** mkscripts MOVES $20 / $21 on close_punch, $23 on close_knee ('approach': the walk-in
  dropped; the move ends back on its first pose), the strike hold given back as the headbutt's
  (EC_LAG_TICKS). Blade's hit steps and damage as before. Recipes and meta for the 11 captures
  (mkrecipes, mkcapmeta --out): every pose exact, palettes from the disc.
- **room.** A fighter's .PL and the scripts after it (player 2's, at most 188416 bytes) had, on main:
  Liu Kang 35.7 KB, Reptile 30.0, Sub-Zero 20.4, Johnny 17.7, Sonya 10.5, Scorpion 9.9, Kano 4.7,
  Raiden 1.4. The moves cost 3.5-7.1 KB; all fit but Raiden's (3.5 KB for the punch alone). No
  near-identical pose of his to merge (one pair under 200 px left). Raiden keeps the far moves up close
  (mkscripts.CLOSE_DIRS). Kano is left with 1.2 KB.
- **found: 12-20 KB of every MK fighter's art is stored twice** - the same image, the same anchor within
  it, from captures taken at other places on the screen (scorpion_full's pose key has the screen anchor).
  Keying on the image and its own anchor drops them (Scorpion 154 -> 140 poses, Raiden -11 KB), but
  changes every fighter's .PL, and some captures are cut by action (the spear's ahead, projectiles
  stripped): its own change, with its own checks. Raiden's close moves would fit after it.
- **Sonya's leg grab** is down + X + A (mkinputs.LEG_BUTTONS; MK CD needs no Block, measured); Z held too
  is cleared with them as before (LEG_CLEARS). The same instructions: her block's size unchanged.
- **checked:** closemovecheck.py (new): all seven, both sides, X / Y / A up close show the close move's
  script frames and hurt Shadow (Raiden: the far ones); expecting the old frames it fails (tried: Scorpion, Kano's
  knee, Sonya; Kano's punches and Raiden pass, unchanged). leggrabcheck: with and without Z. Enlarged in-game frames of every
  fighter's close punch and knee beside MK's: the same poses, the hit on the victim. The 12-fight
  frame-by-frame record: EC 2-player fights and the CPU Raiden fight identical to main's; the fights with
  another CPU MK fighter start a frame or two apart (his .PL loads longer, so the duel state is saved at
  another moment: Shadow's first move differs at frame 2) and his close moves are new, so they can't be
  compared frame for frame.
- **found, the full checks (153 of 156): MK's close moves are short, EC's close range isn't.** EC takes
  X / Y / A as the close moves out to ~70 px (Blade's own reach), and MK's close strikes reach ~52: at
  FINISH HIM the dazed loser left 60-66 px away by the jab was never finished (X, Y hit nothing; Kano's
  headbutt did the same on main). The close strike frames' boxes now reach as far as the far move's
  they replace (scorpion_full CLOSE_REACH: the pictures are MK's, only the box is longer); Raiden's and
  Shang's art as before. closemovecheck has a FINISH HIM part: Shadow dazed where the jab left her
  (50-66 px), X, Y and A each finish her, both sides. mkfatalitycheck waits for the winner's punch to
  end before the input (MK CD's close punch holds its strike ~40 frames: an input in it is lost, as on MK).
- **found: a quick fatality tap was lost at 1 timing in 7 (on main too).** The fatality readers (Scorpion's,
  and the one the others share) run every other frame, at times every third, and EC's pad word
  ($A3A / $A3C: SUB.BIN $6552 writes it, $A42 / $A44 the one before, $A3E / $A40 the new presses) shows a
  3-frame tap for a frame or two - a press shows 0-2 frames late, a release at once. A tap between two
  calls was never seen (EC's new presses, $162, miss it the same way). Both readers now also take the pad
  a frame before: Scorpion's Up in the saved word's sign (his block had 2 bytes left), the shared one's
  directions new since its last call (+44, kept every call). mkfatalitycheck at 14 timings
  (waits 20-33), all seven fighters: 98 of 98 (before: Scorpion 30, Liu Kang 30, Sub-Zero 12 lost); as
  player 2, Sub-Zero / Liu Kang / Kano at 7: all. allchecks runs Scorpion and Liu Kang at 7 timings.
- **seen: an unfinished FINISH HIM ends by EC's time-up, the loser standing on 1.** The CPU Sub-Zero in
  eitherpunchcheck now slides Shadow dazed from ~190 px and walks off (his fight starts a frame apart
  with the bigger .PL); 125 calls after FINISH HIM the round clock is set to 1, it reads 00, and ~250
  frames later EC gives him the round: SUB ZERO WINS CONTEST, the stats (seen on screen; the same on
  main with a human winner who stands still). The check took Shadow on 0 as the only end: now his win
  counts too. (MK: the loser collapses. Ours stands through it, as noted under Sound above.)
- **duelstate** saves its state whole (a temp file per process, then renamed): checks run in parallel
  make the same one.

## Liu Kang's flying kick at MK's speed (2026-11-24)

Asked for: "the speed of Liu Kang's flying kick... in slow motion almost"; and Raiden's torpedo.

- **measured on MK CD** (P1 world x $7BE34, mkmoves' states): the flying kick flies 7.5 px a frame,
  level; Raiden's torpedo 7.5. Ours: the torpedo 7 (fine), the kick 2.5 - flykick_plan spread
  FLYKICK_DIST (196 px) over the capture's 38-frame hold. Now FLYKICK_SPEED, 15 px a tick (the dash
  header's speed is per tick of 2 frames).
- **MK on contact** (recorded, enlarged frames): he doesn't fly on. He stays in the kick, floats back
  ~56 px in 25 frames, holds ~13 more, and lands 38 frames after the contact. At the new speed ours
  carried Shadow ~280 px across the stage. emit_flykick_stop (Liu Kang's block): in the flight step,
  once the hit is spent ($F7 bit 1: hit or blocked), his velocity ($C6, applied while the step's $0400
  flag is on, and $5E, this tick's) becomes 6 px a tick backwards - $C6 is on the screen, so by his
  facing ($20 bit 11) - and the step is held 16 ticks more (task +$26). Probed first: the step's dx
  ($E6) applies once, not every tick; clearing $0400 stops him dead. EC slows the drift to a stop by
  itself (~21 frames), as MK's. Measured: 51 px back in 21 frames, then still, the kick held 38
  frames, landing (MK: 56 in 25, 38).
- **found while testing:** a kick that catches a jumping victim gets EC's own velocity at the hit (+7,
  not the flight's 15): the first version, which tested for the flight's speed, flew on. It tests the
  direction now (going backwards already: done).
- **checked:** flykickcheck (both sides now): 7.0-8.0 px a frame, 7-8 px on after the hit, 42-43 back,
  the kick held 32-38 frames, the move over 42-48 frames after the hit. From 270 px, standing, ducking
  and jumping victims: all float back. eitherpunchcheck (Liu Kang), fightcheck cpu / duel 17,
  mkfatalitycheck liukang, closemovecheck liukang: ok.

## Each MK pose stored once; Raiden's close punch (2026-11-24)

Asked for: "if you are sure identical art is stored twice we should optimize... it is critical we don't
mess up the animation".

- **the duplicates:** scorpion_full keyed a pose on its image and its anchor on the screen, so the same
  image from captures taken at other places was stored once per place. Now a second pass after Raiden's
  merges stores it once: the same pixels, the crop's size (the first try keyed the bytes alone - two
  crops of other shapes could have matched), the anchor within the crop, the same palette, and not
  across the actions cut or boxed their own way (OWN_CUT: the spear, the projectiles; FIT_ALL; the
  uppercut the sweep's box reads). Nothing near-identical is merged.
- **why that is safe:** pose_pieces cuts a pose by its position relative to its own image (scale_pose
  crops it first), and everything after the cut reads it, its action only for the classes kept apart.
- **proved, not argued:** tools/dev/artproof.py builds every MK fighter's .PL with the builder before and
  after, decodes every frame of every sheet from the built file with EC's own unpacker (ecunpack) and
  compares the tile bytes and the record (boxes, points, pieces). All nine, 380 frames each: none
  differ. (plsheet.render, through plmap, drew some frames scrambled in both versions - tiles another
  frame loads: not a proof; ecunpack is the unpacker the build already checks every bank with.)
  Room back: Kano 8.9 KB, Reptile 7.4, Sub-Zero 7.2, Raiden 5.5, Sonya 3.7, Johnny 3.4, Scorpion 2.1,
  Liu Kang and Shang 0 (fewer poses, his .PL not grown).
- **Raiden's close punch** (captured: mkmoves close_punch / close_knee, enlarged poses checked; recipe
  exact, 20 of 20): with the 5.5 KB it fits; the knee too would be 626 bytes over, so up close his A is
  still his far low kick (mkscripts.CLOSE_PUNCH_ONLY). artproof for Raiden ran with the punch taken
  out (both builders then build the same moves): none differ. In the game: lean, wind-up, strike,
  Shadow hit, back the same way (enlarged frames); closemovecheck: all seven ok, his FINISH HIM part too.

## MK CD's other stages, and which stage a fight is on (2026-11-24)

Asked for: MK CD's remaining stages in the game, at random, "only when one or both fighters are MK
people, otherwise if it's two EC people the stage will be picked ... how it's picked in EC".

- **MK's own 2-player order** (tools/capture/mkstagestates.py, 8 battles, each battle's sound matched
  against every MK track): Courtyard (MK track 4), Palace Gates (6), Warrior Shrine (3), the Pit (4),
  Throne Room (7), Goro's Lair (3), then round again. BACKGD1-6.BIN are those six, in that order.
- **The art** (tools/lib/mkstage_disc.py): each stage's tiles and palette lines 2-3 from its BACKGDn;
  where MK puts each tile (its maps go in at offsets, its floors are laid by code) measured once as
  name tables from the battle states (tools/capture/mkstagemaps.py -> data/mk_stages/, numbers only).
  --check: all four identical to MK's planes (palette line 2 entry 9 set by the fight, $21, as the
  Lair's; MK's HUD rows aside). MK animates the Courtyard's monks (12 tiles, every 8 frames) and the
  Shrine's torches (28 tiles): ours show the disc's frame.
- **fixed (RNC):** mkpitbottom.rnc1 read a block that ends on an odd byte at the file's end
  (BACKGD5's last) as if that byte were 0: the last match came out 131 long instead of 227, the
  output 96 bytes short, and the next "chunk" ran off the file forever. All 22 BACKGD blocks now pass
  their own CRC, and rnc1 checks every block's CRC.
- **How EC picks a stage** (GAME.CO, every fight starts with jmp $6C98, d0 = the stage mode, after
  putting its own return address in $143C(a6)): the Contest's matches ($149B6) the opponent's home
  stage (GAME $C28 by slot); the Eternal Champion and the Dark Champion ($14B44, $14BCA) their arenas
  $12 / $11; the Duel ($14DA6) its BACKGROUND SETTING ($1CFC: 0 RANDOM - a shuffled list of every
  unlocked fighter's home, 1-13 a stage by name, 14 PLAYER 2 - player 2's home); the Tournament
  ($18A52) a random one of 13 (GAME $18A98), never twice running. $1438 is no game: 7 one player, 2
  two, in all three.
- **The pick** (tools/lib/mkstages.py): $6C98 jumps to it first. Bosses' arenas stay. Two EC
  fighters: EC's choice - but the Pit (an MK slot's home, from the Duel's RANDOM) becomes EC's own
  random stage. An MK fighter on either side: one of MK's six at random, never the last one again;
  except a Duel set to a stage by name (that stage, so EC's Overkills can still meet MK fighters) or
  PLAYER 2 with an EC player 2 (his home), and Reptile's Contest match (the Pit, as MK's).
  tools/checks/stagepickcheck.py runs the built pick under unicorn through every case.
- **Five stages in one stage mode:** Chin Wo's ($16, JET.BG/BGI) is nobody's home since his slot is
  Raiden's and in no list. JET.BG and JET.BGI become packs (each stage's part from a sector start);
  mode $16's init (778 bytes in SUB.BIN) becomes a dispatcher: for the stage picked it sets the camera
  limits, the stage card's entry ($B0CC + 4 * 5), the stage's CD track ($AC12 by stage) and EC's file
  table entries for JET.BG/BGI ($12E9E + 8 * file id: sector, sectors) - before the card and loads,
  which every init does in the same order (camera, $AC38: card and round set-up, the loads unless a
  round restarts, $AB7 bit 5) - then jumps to the rest of that stage's init, in its own BGI (resident
  all fight). disc.stale_files now takes a file table entry inside its file as fine.
- **The rest of the init is generated** from MK's camera (tools/capture/mkstagecamera.py: walking both
  fighters to each stop - 900 frames each way; 360 didn't reach the Throne Room's): one layer object per
  scroll band with art, x mode 4. **found:** x mode 4 moves a layer by (camera - x3c) * speed / 1024 on
  top of the camera's own movement, so MK's rate r is speed (r - 1) * 1024 (first try, speed r * 1024:
  every band at 1 + r). **found:** with the camera's y at 16, plane B's VSRAM stayed 0 while its bands'
  lines were taken 16 up (each band scrolled 16 lines of the next one's art); MK's camera never moves
  up or down, so ours stays at y 0. Each band's art is MK's plane rows unwrapped from where MK's
  camera starts. A layer per band; Chin Wo's BG has 7 layer ids ($44-$4A, ids are numbered across
  stages), the Lair's steps merge to fit.
- **The floor:** MK's fighters' lowest pixel on each stage (Courtyard 214, Gates 217, Shrine 213,
  Throne 202, Lair 212); ours stand 6 px below $1496 with the camera's y at 0. MK draws no shadows on
  any stage: the shadow test (patches.pit_no_shadows' routine) now in the dispatcher's space, with
  mode $16.
- **Music:** MK tracks 3, 6, 7 added as tracks 20-22 (cdaudio.STAGE_TRACKS); the Courtyard plays 4,
  the Pit's track (13). Track 19 ends with 60 s of silence: it's no longer the disc's last.
  **found:** the old note "EC freezes mid-fight on a disc with 20 tracks" doesn't hold: a cold-booted
  20-track disc and the 19-track one, the same scripted fights, KO to the next round in 620 / 621
  frames, both matches through to the stats. (Likely a state saved on the 19-track disc loaded on
  the other.)
- **Cards:** the stage's name on line 1, line 2 empty. **open:** EC's card font has no apostrophe:
  GORO'S LAIR shows as GORO S LAIR.
- **checked:** mkstagecheck (all five: music, feet, camera range, every band's travel between stops
  within 1 px, background vs MK's at both stops within the colour fit: Throne and Lair 0.1%, Courtyard
  5%, Gates 7%, Shrine 8% - EC leaves a stage 23 colours to MK's 30); each stage's music in a fight
  matched MK's track 0.96-0.99; contestcheck as Scorpion, 6 matches: the Pit, the Lair, the Pit (its
  fall), Courtyard, Shrine, Courtyard, no freeze.
- **open:** MK's animations - the Courtyard's monks swaying, the Shrine's torches, the Lair's eyes
  blinking, the Throne Room's Shang (its frames: BACKGD5's sixth block). MK's FIGHT! and FINISH HIM /
  FINISH HER zoom text.
- **fixed (mine, caught by finishmusiccheck):** patches.cd_times finds the cues counting back from the
  end of track 19's file; the 60 s tail after them moved every cue a minute on (FINISH HIM played the
  losing music). It counts the tail now.
- **fixed (check):** contestcheck --throws threw with toward + Y next to the Dark Champion: up close
  that's MK's close punch now, so it threw only when the random rolls kept him from blocking (the MK
  stages' pick draws a random number, and the boss fight played out otherwise: he blocked it all
  fight). It throws with B up close, MK CD's throw: thrown in 13-14 s whatever stage came first.

## MK's stage animations, and MK's FIGHT! / FINISH HIM (2026-11-25)

- **What MK animates** (tools/capture/mkstagemaps.py records it, 1500 frames, both fighters still):
  the Courtyard's monks (12 tiles' pixels, 4 states, about every 8 frames: the second state is
  BACKGD1.BIN $214A), the Gates' two torches (19 name-table cells each, 8 states), the Lair's eyes
  (16 cells: 7 groups, 5-20 states; the closing frames are BACKGD6's sixth block). Nothing on the
  Shrine or the Throne Room in 1500 frames (the "28 animated tiles" there were MK's fighter tiles
  past the stage's range). Each element's states, MK's own sequence (looped: ANIM_LOOP 900 frames),
  in data/mk_stages/<stage>.json; their pixels from the disc (mkstage_disc.py: anim.json).
- **Ours:** an animated cell gets a tile of its own (cells with the same art in every state share
  one - the monks' row is 12 tiles, as MK's - each element's tiles together), one palette line for
  all its states; the stage code runs a task that queues each state's tiles for VRAM when MK's
  sequence says. **found:** $CE1A queues a VRAM transfer (d0 source, d1 VRAM address, d2 bytes: a
  source in PRG RAM is copied to Word RAM first); the stages' tile slot 9 is at VRAM tile 708. A
  sleep lasts 2 frames (the task counts down 2, keeping the remainder).
- **MK's text** (tools/capture/mktext.py -> data/mk_text.json): PAC.PRG $1DDC2 holds 11 big letters
  (F I G H T N S M E R !, 32 x 32, 4 x 4 tiles each); MK redraws one letter every 2 frames at the
  zoom of that moment (about 5 sizes a letter as it grows, top-left anchored) and spreads them;
  red (palette line 2), a grey flash at full size (line 1). Each frame's letters, places and sizes
  measured (sizes by matching PAC's letters scaled).
- **Ours:** a raw sprite sheet in place of Chin Wo's sheet 2 and its tile source 2 (raw sheets'
  tiles stay in RAM and go to the object's own VRAM block when it shows a frame: SUB $FB12); sizes
  to the nearest 8 px, each frame its letter's bounding box, the timelines after the frames; a
  director task: FIGHT! when bit 6 of $28001 rises (EC sets it as BEGIN CONTEST ends), FINISH HIM /
  HER when a fighter is dazed ($1F) on 1 health (mkinputs.FEMALE_MASK); the letters are spawned
  then (an object each, a 16-tile VRAM block: $6942) and removed after ($82 bit 2).
  **found** on the way: nine letters spawned at the round's start left EC's BEGIN CONTEST waiting
  forever (objects); a task's registers don't last a sleep (EC's sleep keeps the task's block in
  a4: the first director used a4 and froze at BEGIN CONTEST) - all its state is in memory now;
  the director's variables sit in the stage's code, which lasts the match - round 2's director found
  round 1's FIGHT! done (each clears its own now); between rounds the fight flag comes on for 6
  frames - FIGHT! waits for it to stay on 4 ticks (it shows 8-11 frames after the fight begins);
  the HUD's colours (line 1, 10-15) change at FINISH HIM - the text uses the stage's line 0 only,
  with MK's red put in the palette where the stage has none near (the Gates, the Throne Room, the
  Lair: one colour of 23). Room: a stage's .BG may reach $43000 (the CD loader's stack there as it
  loads: patches.cd_ai_stack): mkstages.BG_MAX, the Throne Room's 48836 of 49152.
- **left out:** MK's grey flash of the full-size letters (11 x 16 more tiles than the BG has room
  for: red throughout); the Pit has no text (its .BG, 45 KB with the pit's bottom, has no room);
  EC's MATCH 01 and BEGIN CONTEST still come first (FIGHT! follows); GORO'S LAIR's card still
  reads GORO S LAIR (EC's card font - MAIN.BIN's, ranges of characters to glyphs - has no apostrophe).
- **checked:** mktextcheck's round 2 (round 1 lost: FIGHT! again at round 2's start, not at the blink),
  mkstagecheck (animations: each element's tiles change as often as MK's sequence and
  show only its states; backgrounds with any state allowed: Courtyard 5.6 / 6.4%, Gates 7.4 / 7.9%,
  Shrine 7.6 / 8.1%, Throne 0.1%, Lair 0.1 / 0.2%), mktextcheck (all five: FIGHT! at the fight's
  start and the fight goes on; FINISH HER on Shadow, FINISH HIM on Larcen; gone after), MK's and
  ours side by side (torches, monks, eyes; FINISH HER / HIM letter by letter, enlarged).

## The spear flies as far as MK's (2026-11-25)

- **MK CD** (a Scorpion vs Sonya duel, thrown from 131 to 233 px apart: the widest the screen allows)
  hits from any distance. The rope is sprites of its own: in flight a chain of ~15 wavy 16 x 16
  pieces (one 32 px wave repeated) from the screen's edge, through Scorpion, to the head, sliding
  ~7.5 px a frame from ~89 px in front of him; on a hit a straight line of 24 x 8 pieces from behind
  him to the victim, shaking 4 px, held ~35 frames, then the pull at ~6 px a frame; a crouching
  victim isn't hit (it flies on off the screen).
- **Ours** had the rope and head drawn in his poses and the hit on the hand's strike box: a box is
  signed bytes, so 127 px at most (it hit from ~140). Now (tools/lib/mkspear.py,
  mkinputs.emit_spear_rope): the rope is an object of its own, on a raw sheet in his .PL ($F6,
  source $126; Reptile's $F7 / $127), art cut from MK's capture of the throw (the wave behind him,
  the head in front, the straight rope), his poses without it (mkspear.strip_rope: what is thinner
  than 4 px). Thrown at his release frame (42:2E), held there while it flies; it's his projectile
  to EC ($6A38) with the spear's attack ($78 = $031D, record $1D), its hit box at the head and the
  rope's line (over a crouch). The hand's hit (42:2F's attack event) and strike box are gone.
- **found** on the way:
  - EC's raw draw gives every piece tiles of its own in the object's VRAM block (SUB $FD20: one
    run after another), copied each time the frame changes - pieces showing the same tiles don't
    share them. The first rope (8 pieces of 12 tiles, a 27-tile block) ran off VRAM's top and on
    over plane A's map at $0000: junk tiles over the sky. Now its frames are the head and the waves
    back to his hand only (at most 52 tiles: the block), the wave 2 tiles high (a pixel off its peak
    and trough); spawned on an empty frame, and empty while EC gives it no block ($7B bit 7).
  - EC removes a projectile as it hits when $7D bit 7 is set (PLYR $1A030): cleared, the rope stays
    on him. For a frame or two after the hit the victim isn't in his hit state yet ($8000): the rope
    waits ROPE_GRACE runs before it looks.
  - EC's projectile hit puts Scorpion's victim in state $E ($800E: bit 15 EC's flag), not a reel
    ($1A-$1D; Reptile's victim reels): the pull (r_drag), the miss check (emit_spear_miss) and the
    rope take $E as the spear's hit; r_drag, whose in-the-air mark comes from the melee hit (r_hit),
    tests the floor itself for it (no pull or daze in the air: EC's landing loop, a hang).
  - The raw draw takes its palette bits from the pieces' attribute words ($6000, as mkprojectile's)
    XORed with the object's $20 (his).
  - From far, the 22-frame hold and the longer pull outlast EC's hit stun (the victim walked free as
    the yank ended, 48 frames after the hit): the rope cuts the hold's steps to a tick each once the
    calls since the hit and the pull's add up to ROPE_STUN; Reptile's pull is half again as fast from
    150 px (his reeling victim is let go sooner).
  - Scorpion's block was full (2 bytes): the rope's code is in his .PL's tail (SCO_TAIL, as his
    scripts; position-free), and his move-script table moved there too to make room for the call;
    Reptile's likewise (GEN_TAIL).
- **checked:** spearcheck (both, from 70 to 215 px: hit, pulled in, dazed within EC's stun; not hit
  crouching), airspearcheck, spearsoundcheck; blocked (no damage, the bare hold), from 45 px, facing
  left; enlarged frames (the wave from his hand to the head, the straight rope as he pulls).

## MK's announcer: FIGHT! (2026-11-25)

- **Where it could go:** a fighter's voice bank (each MK fighter's is 15.6-17.6 KB of its 17.7 and has its
  rate lowered to fit already: FIGHT.SVX, 4.6 KB at MK's 8025 Hz, would have meant lowering every voice;
  Scorpion's has no voice id left) or FIGHT.SND (63 of 63 pages). Neither.
- **found** Sound RAM in a fight: FIGHT.SND from 0, player 1's voice bank from $3F00, player 2's after
  (the .SNDs' second PCM chunks - the Overkill / Vendetta sounds - load only for those, from the .OC / .VC
  modules). The biggest pair (two EC banks of 79 pages) ends at $DD00: above it, ~8.9 KB nobody loads into.
- **found** The fight's banks (SUB $ACEE): each .SND read into $4A000 / $4C800, its chunks registered by EC's
  loader (types $1C / $20 at -$6E6C(a6) + their +$A word: SUB $6630 / $6674 - the stage files' chunks too,
  but nothing hands a stage's to the driver), then the driver's load ($47776: a0 the table chunk's +$C -
  its entry offsets made absolute -, a1 the PCM chunk's +$C) and $47696, which keeps where it ended: the
  next $47776 goes on from there. $4890A (called first at the fight's start and by the Overkills) keys
  every channel off; $47776 itself only clears a scratch table ($48AAE). Chunk types $C, $10 and $14 are
  rts in the loader (SUB $66F6-$66FA).
- **done** A sound bank (one table entry, id $3F6 - no .SND has it -, MK's FIGHT.SVX at its own rate) in
  each MK stage's code, after the text director (mkstages.fight_voice_bank); the director hands it to
  $47776 on its first tick each round (the fighters' banks are in by then) and plays $3F6 as FIGHT!'s
  letters start. The Pit (no room for the letters) does it from its watch (ecstage.pit_fight_voice): at
  the fight flag's first tick the load, at its fourth the voice. It lands after player 2's bank ($B200,
  $D800 for Sub-Zero vs Dawson); nobody's bank changed.
- **room:** the Palace Gates' code was too full (its torches' 16 states and their runs): runs are bytes
  now (state, frames: half the size), each state found through a pointer table, and what doesn't fit goes
  in the BG in a chunk the loader passes over (type $10) before the map - not after it: the AI loads over
  the BG from $40800. The Gates' BG 49042 of 49152, BGI 12200 of 12288.
- **checked:** fightvoicecheck (all six: in sound RAM, the banks whole; the Courtyard's and the Pit's
  round 2 heard - with the play call and without, the difference is FIGHT.SVX, correlation 0.91); round 1
  from the menu (the Courtyard, the Throne Room: the voice ~10 frames after the fight begins, with the
  letters); mkstagecheck, mktextcheck, soundbankcheck, pitcheck, pitstartcheck.

## The spear thrown at a cornered fighter (2026-11-25)

- **found** (the gate: Scorpion's Contest stuck at match 2, Rax backed into the Courtyard's left end, ~45 px
  away) Thrown up close from there the rope's head starts past the arena's end, the rope was gone, and
  his throw - held on its release frame while the rope flies - was held for good: neither could finish.
- **fixed** The hold looks at the rope each call (still his: its sheet; not taken: $82 bit 2) and lasts
  ROPE_FLY_MAX calls at most; without it his throw goes on (the miss's bare hold). The rope's hit box
  reaches back from the head to 1 px in front of him as it's thrown (MK hits a cornered fighter too),
  still only at the rope's height (a crouch is passed over). spearcheck throws at Shadow cornered: hit,
  his throw over.

## The spear on MK's timeline (2026-11-25)

- **found** (tools/dev/spearcmp.py: MK CD's spear and ours from 71, 131 and 220 px, side by side) Ours threw the
  rope ~14 frames late: the release was the script's tenth step, after six steps of poses MK shows while the
  rope is already flying. The 20 spear frames spread evenly over MK's 35 poses put his two-handed pull pose
  on the flight and the hold. And MK pins the victim where the rope hits him ~34 frames at any distance,
  bleeding, then yanks him in at ~6.5 px a frame; ours started pulling almost at once - EC's projectile hit
  lets a fighter go ~26 frames after it and walks him free by ~48, so the hold had been cut to 22 frames
  (shorter still from far).
- **found** That stun is the victim's move task: +$3A the hit script's step's ticks, +$1C the stun ($10C is
  a copy: holding it did nothing). Both held, he stays in the hit on one pose as long as wanted.
- **fixed** mkinputs.spear_timeline: the wind-up seven steps a tick each, step 7 the release (42:2E), the hold
  34 frames from the hit, the yank's steps on its poses in MK's order, no forward lunge (Scorpion's script and
  Reptile's). scorpion_full.SPEAR_POSES: MK's pose for each spear frame - eight, as before (his .PL and its
  code tail had no room for more: 11 were 3 KB over). The rope's code (sr_h2, in the .PL tail) holds the
  victim's two counters while the rope is in him, sprays blood from his chest every 16 frames, holds the
  yank's last frame till he's in, and lets him go there (his stun ends at once: the daze follows). The far
  hold cut, ROPE_STUN and Reptile's faster far pull are gone.
- **checked** spearcheck (rewritten: hit within 50 frames of the press, pinned 26+ frames, pulled in and dazed;
  Scorpion 37, Reptile 35 frames pinned from 70 to 215 px), and the gate.

## The launcher app (2026-11-26)

- **why** Players on Windows (or anyone without a terminal) couldn't build the game. tools/launcher: a window
  that finds the player's two discs and BIOS (or takes each), builds, plays it in RetroArch, or saves the disc
  for their own emulator. One file to download: a Windows .exe (PyInstaller around a portable Python) or a
  Linux AppImage, packaged by .github/workflows/launcher.yml. No game data in either.
- **pinned** Python 3.12.16 (python-build-standalone 20261124), the build's packages at the dev venv's
  versions (Pillow 12.1.1, numpy 2.3.5, scipy 1.16.3, unicorn 2.1.4, capstone 5.0.9), RetroArch 1.22.2 and its
  cores pack (SHA-256 each; only Genesis Plus GX is unpacked), appimagetool 1.9.1. A new RetroArch changes
  nothing until retroarch.PINNED moves.
- **checked** (Linux) The AppImage's Python identified both discs and built a data track identical to the
  working copy's (6d498b68...). RetroArch from the launcher boots the disc with the BIOS; its home is kept in
  the launcher's folder (run plainly it made ~/.config/retroarch), its AppImage unpacked once (not to /tmp
  each run). Windows: only on GitHub's machines (selftest, prepare.py --list); a real build there is the
  player's first.
